<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at every fixed inverse temperature <i>β</i> &gt; 1, we prove that the standard deviation of the log partition function is $`n^{1/6+o(1)}`$. The same exponent describes its typical centered absolute fluctuations, establishing the predicted one-sixth exponent in this regime.

- **claim-low-temperature-fluctuation-exponent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $1<\beta<\infty$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{sd}}(F_n)=n^{1/6+o(1)},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(F_n)=n^{1/3+o(1)}.$$ More explicitly, for every $\eta>0$ there is $n_0(\beta,\eta)$ such that $$n^{1/6-\eta}\le\mathop{\mathrm{sd}}(F_n)\le n^{1/6+\eta} \qquad(n\ge n_0(\beta,\eta)).$$ Moreover, for every $\eta>0$, $$\mathbb P\!\left(n^{1/6-\eta}\le\|F_n-\mathbb EF_n\| \le n^{1/6+\eta}\right)\longrightarrow1.$$
  - Explanation: Identifies the standard-deviation, variance and typical centered-magnitude power scales for the zero-field Gaussian model at fixed low temperature.
- **claim-source-covariance-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-found-source)): The gradient of a continuation in a source coupled to a bounded feature $\phi$ is the conditional mean of $\phi$. Its Hessian is the sum of the remaining conditional martingale covariance increments, weighted by the mass of each transition, with mass $1$ for the final spin draw. In particular, the Hessian $H_u$ in a normalized spin source satisfies $$uC_u\preceq H_u\preceq C_u.$$ If $y_1,y_2$ are independent continuations from the prefix at $u$, then $$\mathbf E(y_1\cdot y_2)=S_u,\qquad \mathbf E(y_1\cdot y_2)^2=B_u,$$ and hence $D_u$ is the standard deviation of this overlap. Moreover, $$D_u^2=\operatorname{Var}(\|X_u\|^2) +2\mathbf E[X_u^{\mathsf T}C_uX_u] +\mathbf E\operatorname{tr}C_u^2.$$
  - Explanation: Represents source derivatives by conditional feature means and weighted covariance increments, and identifies overlap dispersion.
- **claim-gaussian-pressure-calculus** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-pressure)): The first two lines of <a href="#eq:1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:1">[eq:1]</a> hold for finite hierarchies, including degenerate Gaussian increments by continuity. In addition, for any common external source and any two admissible step paths, $$\|f_n(b,h)-f_n(\widetilde b,\widetilde h)\| \leq\tfrac14\\|b-\widetilde b\\|_{L^1} +\tfrac12\\|h-\widetilde h\\|_{L^1}.$$ The bound is uniform in the source when the same self correction is used.
  - Explanation: Establishes pressure differentiation, susceptibility growth and a source-uniform continuity bound for finite hierarchies.
- **claim-unconditioned-pinned-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-moments)): At zero normalized spin source and with no other deterministic external field, the last two lines of <a href="#eq:1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:1">[eq:1]</a> hold with $c_2=1$ and $c_p\leq1+p^2/3$. All these moments concern one planted path and its terminal spin, with the full, unconditioned prefix law.
  - Explanation: Bounds centered single-path observables under the full unconditioned planted law, using terminal-spin pinning in the proof.
- **claim-real-mass-covariance-differentiation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-found-replica)): Let $K_\theta(u;\sigma,\tau)$ be an affine family of cumulative Gaussian covariance kernels of a finite hierarchy. Assume that the independent increment covariances are positive semidefinite throughout the parameter interval. Let $V=\partial_\theta K_\theta$ and let $P$ be a fixed bounded spin test on a fixed observed split tree. Then $$\frac{d}{d\theta}\nu_\theta[P] =\frac12\sum_{c,d}\nu_\theta[P V_{cd}]. \tag{2}\label{eq:2}$$ The signed sum includes coincidences with old labels and between $c,d$. For distinct labels, $V_{cd}$ is evaluated at their common revelation; on the diagonal it is evaluated at the terminal covariance. The exact weights are the real-power weights described in the proof. A constant diagonal contribution, independent of the summation label, sums to zero. Conditional means in $P$ can be represented by independent additional replicas at their specified prefixes before this formula is applied.
  - Explanation: Differentiates planted expectations through signed allocations of additional labels without an integer-replica assumption.
- **claim-attachment-measure-row-collapse** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-attachment)): Fix an enclosing block of mass $a\in[0,1]$ containing $r\geq1$ observed leaves. After grouping placements which induce the same extended tree, one free summation label has the following signed attachment measure:* 1. *weight $+1$ for coincidence with each observed leaf;* 2. *measure $-dm$ on every occupied open edge, parameterized by its increasing mass coordinate;* 3. *weight $(1-d_v)u_v$ at an observed fork of mass $u_v$ with $d_v$ occupied children, including an initial fork if present.* *The total signed mass is $a$ and the total variation is $2r-a\leq2r$. For an empty block the first label has weight $a$. Consequently, adjoining $k$ labels to a tree with $r\geq1$ old labels has total variation at most $$2^k r(r+1)\cdots(r+k-1).$$ Adjoining $k\geq1$ labels in a previously empty block has variation at most $a\,2^{k-1}(k-1)!$. These bounds are independent of the mass partition. A free row whose summand is independent of the detailed attachment inside a block of mass $u$ collapses exactly to $u$ times that summand.
  - Explanation: Specifies signed attachment weights, their total variation, and the exact collapse of a placement-independent free row.
- **claim-new-split-density-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-split-density)): Fix a finite observed tree with at most $q$ leaves and allocate at most $k$ new labels. Apart from coincidences and atoms at specified old forks or cuts, the split mass of any specified pair involving a new label has a Lebesgue density with total variation bounded by a constant $C_{q,k}$. The same statement holds when some old fork masses are themselves integrated against a measure of bounded density, with the resulting bound multiplied by that density bound. These assertions concern the signed attachment measure and its variation, not the size of a spin observable at a pointwise split level.
  - Explanation: Controls non-atomic split densities while retaining coincidences and specified fork or cut atoms separately.
- **claim-partition-independent-taylor-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-taylor)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:found-replica" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:found-replica">6</a>, suppose $P$ uses at most $q$ labels and $\|V(u;\sigma,\tau)\|\leq M$. For each fixed integer $j\geq1$ there is $C_{q,j}<\infty$, independent of the number of covariance steps, such that $$\left\|\frac{d^j}{d\theta^j}\nu_\theta[P]\right\| \leq C_{q,j}M^j\\|P\\|_\infty.$$ More precisely the derivative is an integral over a signed measure of extended trees with uniformly bounded total variation, of expectations of $P$ times $j$ covariance insertions, with the factor $2^{-j}$. Hölder’s inequality can therefore be applied to these integrands under their genuine probability laws. If $P\geq0$, then $$\left\|\frac{d}{d\theta}\nu_\theta[P]\right\| \leq C_{q,1}M\nu_\theta[P],$$ and hence $$e^{-C_{q,1}M\|\theta-\theta'\|}\nu_{\theta'}[P] \leq\nu_\theta[P] \leq e^{C_{q,1}M\|\theta-\theta'\|}\nu_{\theta'}[P].$$ The assertions require that the test, including any scalar centers in it, be fixed during the interpolation. For parameter-dependent tests the ordinary product-rule derivatives must also be included.
  - Explanation: Bounds fixed-order covariance derivatives independently of the hierarchy mesh and compares nonnegative fixed tests multiplicatively.
- **claim-uniform-source-derivative-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-found-source-bounds)): For fixed $n$, every derivative order of a continuation in bounded feature directions has a bound independent of the mass partition, uniform over the external source and the admissible bounded covariance clocks. The bound depends only on the order and the suprema of the feature directions.
  - Explanation: Controls every fixed source derivative order uniformly over bounded clocks, external sources and mass partitions.
- **claim-grouped-cut-layer-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-cut-truncation)): Fix an old tree with at most $q$ leaves in a block of mass $U$, and a free row label $r$ in that block. Let $U\leq v\leq w\leq\min(2v,1)$. The difference of its signed row tests truncated at $w$ and at $v$ can, after exact grouping of the deeper placements, be written as a signed combination of pairs of planted laws with total coefficient variation at most $C_qv$. In each pair the old-label tree is the same, and only the coupling of $r$ to one occupied descendant subtree after the revelation at $v$ differs. One may take $C_q=12q$.* *The statement remains true when the row is multiplied by a bounded coefficient depending on the fixed old topology, multiplied into the bound by its supremum. For the exact collapse of deeper placements this coefficient, and all scalar centers in the row test, must be independent of $r$’s detailed attachment beyond the truncation cut. Allocation-order symmetry carries the full topology coefficient with it; it does not establish the independence required for a free-row collapse. At each recursion generation, freeze the previously generated topology, its coefficients, and earlier scalar centers before introducing fresh slots. A current coefficient depending on the row’s deeper placement must remain explicit until eliminated or estimated. Only after this row calculation may the enlarged topology be frozen for the next generation.
  - Explanation: Groups deeper placements before estimating a cut-layer difference and states the coefficient-independence conditions required for collapse.
- **claim-centered-coupling-difference** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-coupling)): Consider either pair of laws in Lemma <a href="#lem:found-cut-truncation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:found-cut-truncation">12</a>. Let $\mathcal G$ be the sigma-field generated by their complete common prefix through the prescribed revelation at $v$, the realizations of all old branches outside the affected descendant subtree after $v$, and the deterministic old topology. No information from the continuation of $r$ or from the affected old subtree after $v$ is included in $\mathcal G$. Let $F$ be a function of $r$ and this exterior information, and let $P$ be a function of the old subtree and the exterior information. The two laws have the same conditional marginal law of $F$ given $\mathcal G$ and the same conditional marginal law of $P$ given $\mathcal G$. Write their common conditional means as $\bar F,\bar P$. Their difference therefore obeys $$(\mathbf E_1-\mathbf E_0)[FP] =(\mathbf E_1-\mathbf E_0)[(F-\bar F)(P-\bar P)].$$ If $p^{-1}+p'^{-1}=1$, its absolute value is at most $$\sum_{i=0}^1\\|F-\bar F\\|_{L^p(\mathbf P_i)} \\|P-\bar P\\|_{L^{p'}(\mathbf P_i)}.$$ Each centered norm is at most the corresponding norm of the difference from an independent resampling with the same conditional marginal given $\mathcal G$. Consequently uniform resampling bounds $A_v,B_v$ for these two factors give a bound $2C_qvA_vB_v$ for the full cut layer.
  - Explanation: Uses equality of conditional marginals to center a coupling difference and bound it by unconditional resampling norms.
- **claim-boundary-polynomial-resampling** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-found-boundary-tests)): Fix a cut $U$ and its old descendant blocks. Let $P$ be a polynomial of fixed degree in overlaps between different blocks, centered by fixed scalars, and in products of such overlaps with fresh auxiliary leaves split at specified cuts. Assume that each overlap involving an affected old subtree has its other endpoint outside that subtree. Bounded coefficients may depend on the entire fixed old topology. All scalar centers may be expectations on an earlier tree, provided they are held fixed during the current row interpolation.* *On resampling the affected subtree while retaining its prefix, only the overlap factors crossing its boundary vary. If each such factor has a resampling-difference bound in the required finite $L^p$ norm, and the other factors have their required finite moment bounds, then $P$ has the bound obtained by the product rule, with a constant depending only on its degree, number of monomials, and coefficient bound. In particular this property is preserved by multiplying the current target test by centered overlaps to fresh auxiliary leaves, and by introducing a fresh target block joined to an earlier target by one overlap. Earlier target blocks may have several incident boundary overlaps.
  - Explanation: Transfers boundary-edge resampling estimates to fixed-degree overlap polynomials while keeping topology coefficients and scalar centers fixed.
- **claim-bounded-monotone-path-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-found-limits)): The preceding construction and estimates extend to bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths $b,h$ by step approximation. The limit is independent of the approximations, provided the terminal clocks converge and the before/after clocks at every specified cut converge to the specified values. Planted expectations of bounded spin tests on a fixed finite split tree with cuts before or after jumps, fixed-order source derivatives, the signed calculus, and the mass-integrated pressure and moment formulas have these limits. The susceptibility inequality holds on the continuous parts, with nonnegative total increments of $S$ at jumps. For intermediate cuts and the separate clock contributions inside a filled jump, use approximations retaining the prescribed joint filling; no independence from the choice of that filling is asserted. All constants in the finite-tree variation and Taylor bounds are unchanged.
  - Explanation: Extends the finite hierarchy calculus to bounded monotone paths with specified cut sides and joint jump fillings.
- **claim-constrained-envelope-existence** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-envelope)): For all sufficiently large $n$, the class <a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a> is nonempty and compact in the uniform topology of inverse quantiles. The minimum in <a href="#eq:6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:6">[eq:6]</a> is attained. For an admissible sweep, $\mathcal M_\lambda$ is locally Lipschitz and nondecreasing in $\lambda$, equals the endpoint pressure at $\lambda=0$, and is an upper bound for that pressure. At almost every positive strength its upper derivative per unit $\log\lambda$ is bounded, at any minimizer at that strength, by $$\frac14\int_{u_0}^1 e(r(u)) \bigl[D_u^2+(S_u-r(u))^2\bigr]\,du. \tag{7}\label{eq:7}$$ All moments in this formula are for the changed clocks in <a href="#eq:6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:6">[eq:6]</a>.
  - Explanation: Proves attainment in the constrained inverse-quantile class and controls the increasing minimized envelope along an admissible sweep.
- **claim-contact-cap-floor-geometry** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-geometry)): At a minimizer there is a continuous absolutely continuous potential $H$ such that $H'=JG$ for a strictly positive factor $J$, $\alpha'=M(\alpha)$ on $\{H<0\}$, and $\alpha'=k$ on $\{H>0\}$. On a band where $\alpha+u_*$ varies by a bounded factor, $J$ has bounded distortion after normalization at one endpoint. On the contact set $\{H=0\}$, almost everywhere, $$G=0,\qquad S'\lesssim n^{o(1)},\qquad S\le r. \tag{9}\label{eq:9}$$ On an open cap component $\{H<0\}$ of radius length $\ell$, $$\begin{gathered} \Delta S\lesssim n^{o(1)} (\ell+r_{\min}\mathbf1_{\mathrm{initial}}), \qquad S\le r+\ell,\\ \|S-r\|^2\lesssim n^{o(1)} \left[D^2+\ell^2+r_{\min}^2\mathbf1_{\mathrm{initial}}\right]. \end{gathered} \tag{10}\label{eq:10}$$ Here $\Delta S$ is the increase through the interior of the component. On contact, $(S-r)^2\lesssim D^2$. Every cap has $\ell\lesssim n^{-\delta}L\log n$; if it is contained in a comparable mass band $u\asymp v\gg u_*$, then $v\ell\lesssim n^{-\delta}E/d^3$. A noninitial cap on which $p=0$ throughout satisfies $\|S-r\|\le\ell$. The total floor mass is at most $n^{-\delta}u_*$.
  - Explanation: Describes the minimizer through a variational potential and bounds contact mismatch, cap widths and total floor mass.
- **claim-global-raw-overlap-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-raw-moments)): For every fixed $p\ge2$, throughout the path, including masses below the working interval, $$\\|X_u\\|_p\lesssim_p\sqrt{u+x},\qquad \\|R_{\mathrm{split\ at}\ u}\\|_p\lesssim_p u+x. \tag{12}\label{eq:12}$$ The bounds also hold at intermediate clock revelations, with their specified mass. The norm of a random vector is its Euclidean norm inside the $L^p$ norm.
  - Explanation: Controls conditional-spin means and split overlaps throughout the path, including below the optimization interval, under the preceding regularity assumptions.
- **claim-field-clock-projection** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-projection)): Let $V$ be a vector determined by outside branches and by data at or before their fork with a branch $b$. Conditional on this data, the future of $b$ has its usual planted law. Suppose a field-clock interval after the fork has length $D_h$, with mass at least $v_->0$ throughout. For even fixed $p\ge2$, the part of $V\cdot y_b$ left after projection at the end of the interval satisfies $$\\|V\cdot(y_b-X_{b,\mathrm{end}})\\|_p \le C_p(\log n)^2\frac{\\|V\\|_p}{v_-\sqrt{nD_h}}. \tag{13}\label{eq:13}$$ Here the masses and clock lengths used in the estimate are bounded below by inverse powers of $n$. Differences of two later projections satisfy the same estimate up to a constant.
  - Explanation: Bounds the residual of an exterior anchor against a branch after a positive field-clock interval.
- **claim-postprojection-endpoint-changes** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-resampling)): Suppose two leaves share only through a mass at most a fixed multiple of $x_1$, where $x_1\ge x$. If a projection or resampling is made after a regular radius $v\ge Cx_1$, with comparable intervals fitting after the fork and before that projection, the change in their inner product caused by changing one endpoint has norm $$\frac{C_p(\log n)^C}{\sqrt{ne(v)v^2}} \tag{16}\label{eq:16}$$ in every fixed $L^p$. For an already projected anchor of norm $O_p(\sqrt{x_1})$, the bound gains a factor $\sqrt{x_1/v}$.
  - Explanation: Controls overlap changes under projection or conditional resampling after a regular innovation interval.
- **claim-deterministic-all-moment-good-window** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-good-split)): Suppose a regular band of mass and radius of order $v\ge Cx$ contains a mass interval of length comparable to $v$ on which $S\asymp v$. There are constants $c_1,c_2>0$, independent of each fixed moment order, and a deterministic window of mass length $n^{-c_1}v$ with the following properties. Its fixed-factor enlargement has $S$ oscillation at most $n^{-c_1/2}v$, and for every deterministic split in a fixed central fraction of the window, $$\\|R_*-S_*\\|_p\le C_p n^{-c_2}v \tag{17}\label{eq:17}$$ for all fixed $p\ge2$. The window may be selected before the moment orders or any later fixed recursion depth are chosen. The constant $c_1$ may be any sufficiently small positive constant.
  - Explanation: Selects a deterministic split window with polynomial overlap concentration before the later fixed moment orders and recursion depth are chosen.
- **claim-local-overlap-second-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-direct-moments)): On every regular outer dyad of mass $u\asymp v$ beyond a sufficiently large fixed multiple of $x$, for every sufficiently small fixed $\epsilon>0$, $$\left(\operatorname{avg}_{u\asymp v}D_u^2\right)^{1/2} \lesssim_\epsilon n^\epsilon\left[ (ne(v)v)^{-1/3}+(ne(v)v^2)^{-1/2} +(n^{-\delta}E/d^3)^{1/2}+n^{\delta/2}u_*\right]. \tag{18}\label{eq:18}$$ Fixed enlargements of a dyad are permitted in this assertion.* *For a small direct test impose additionally $e(x)\gtrsim x^2$, and set $W=\max(x,\sqrt{e(x)})$. Then $W\lesssim d$ and $e(v)\gtrsim W^2$ for $v=O(W)$. On regular dyads of scale at least a fixed multiple of $W$, including comparable dyads at $W$ that lie in the regular region, the right side of <a href="#eq:18" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:18">[eq:18]</a> is polynomially smaller than $x^2/W$. Between $Cx$ and $O(W)$ it is polynomially smaller than $x^2/(W^2v)^{1/3}$. Moreover $$\int_0^{CW}D_u\,du=o(x^2),\qquad D=o(x). \tag{19}\label{eq:19}$$ The integral is truncated at mass $1$, and $C$ may be any fixed constant. The second assertion means normalized mean-square convergence on every compact band $r\le Cx$, $cx\le u\le Cx$, both in mass and in radius: $$x^{-3}\int_{\{r\le Cx,\,cx\le u\le Cx\}}D^2\,du\longrightarrow0, \qquad x^{-3}\int_{\{r\le Cx,\,cx\le\alpha(r)\le Cx\}}D^2\,dr \longrightarrow0.$$ If $W=o(1)$, there is a split $*$ in a regular band of scale $W$ with $S_*\asymp W$, satisfying <a href="#eq:17" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:17">[eq:17]</a>, and with $D_*$ polynomially smaller than $x^2/W$. It can be chosen above any fixed multiple of $x$.
  - Explanation: Bounds overlap dispersion on regular outer dyads and establishes the integrated mass and radius estimates needed for direct tests.
- **claim-conditional-comparison-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-conditional-error)): Let $\lambda\ge n^{-10}$ and $A=A_\lambda=n^\delta\sqrt n E/\sqrt\lambda$. If $A=o(1)$, assume that a minimizer has a regular outer region above $A$. Then its integral <a href="#eq:7" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:7">[eq:7]</a> is at most $n^{10\delta}E$ for all sufficiently large $n$. If $A$ stays bounded below by a positive constant, the same conclusion holds without a regularity hypothesis.* *Consequently, if these hypotheses hold along an admissible sweep from zero to any strength at most $d^2$, then $0\le\mathcal M_\lambda-f_n(b_0,h_0)\le n^{20\delta}E$. The fixed-strength assertions apply also to the wall variant, whereas this last conclusion requires an admissible sweep.
  - Explanation: Bounds the fixed-strength comparison error under outer regularity when needed, and integrates it only when the entire sweep is admissible.
- **claim-whole-unit-trace-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-unit-trace)): Consider a group at comparable masses $u\asymp v$ of total mass at most $Cv$, and suppose the radii and the raw overlap moments in that group are bounded at scale $y_0$. Away from the floor, take as units complete cap components with comparable endpoint masses, and mass elements of the contact set. Let $e_g$ be a lower bound on $e$ in the group. If a cap has no initial-wall loss, then within each unit $$\operatorname{avg}_{\mathrm{unit}} \mathbb E\operatorname{tr}H_u^2 \lesssim\frac{n^{o(1)}}{ne_g}. \tag{20}\label{eq:20}$$ On contact this holds pointwise almost everywhere. In particular the unit averages of $\mathbb E\operatorname{tr}C_u^2$ and $\mathbb EW_u$ are at most $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv^2)$ and $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv)$, respectively.
  - Explanation: Bounds averaged Hessian traces on complete cap units and contact elements, with covariance and pinned-energy consequences.
- **claim-two-sided-level-averaging** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-bracket)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#comp:unit-trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:unit-trace">26</a>, let $\ell_0$ be a common polynomial-scale upper bound for cap widths, including a possible wall term when it is covered by the trace estimate. Suppose on the group, for every fixed $p$, the raw bounds are $\\|X\\|_p\le C_p\sqrt{y_0}$ and $\\|R\\|_p\le C_py_0$. Then, up to a factor $n^{O(\epsilon')}$ for any fixed $\epsilon'>0$, and an edge error $O(k_0n^{-10})$, $$\int_{\mathrm{group}}D_u^2\,du \lesssim v\left[ (y_0/k_0)^2+y_0\ell_0+\ell_0^2 +\frac{k_0+1}{ne_gv^2}\right], \qquad 1\le k_0\le n^2. \tag{21}\label{eq:21}$$ In particular the first and last variable terms may be replaced by $C[(y_0/(ne_gv^2))^{2/3}+1/(ne_gv^2)]$.
  - Explanation: Combines whole-unit traces with ordered side averages to bound integrated overlap dispersion while retaining cap sampling units.
- **claim-uniform-one-site-scalar-closure** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cavity-closure)): Fix $0<c<C<\infty$. At a target cut $i$ satisfying $r_i\le Cx$ and $cx\le u_i\le Cx$, along every sequence with $D_i=o(x)$, $$\label{eq:22}\tag{22} S_i=\Gamma(K_i)+o(x^3).$$ The bound is $O(x^3)$, uniformly over these targets, if $D_i=O(x)$. More precisely, for a fixed direct-test sequence there are $\eta_n\to0$, $C_0<\infty$, and $\vartheta>0$, independent of the target in this band, such that $$\frac{\|S_i-\Gamma(K_i)\|}{x^3} \le C_0\{\eta_n+(D_i/x)^\vartheta\}.$$ When $W=O(x)$, the $O(x^3)$ assertion also holds at every omitted lower cut, without a lower bound on its mass and without <a href="#eq:4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:4">[eq:4]</a> at that cut. On the bands in <a href="#eq:19" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:19">[eq:19]</a>, the error in <a href="#eq:22" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:22">[eq:22]</a>, divided by $x^3$, tends to zero in normalized $L^1$, in both mass and radius coordinates.
  - Explanation: Identifies the local mean overlap with the scalar recursion for the full effective clock path, with a uniform modulus and integrated coordinate versions.
- **claim-bounded-gaussian-row-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-row-change)): Along a segment of admissible changes of one Gaussian row with uniformly bounded covariance kernels, expectations of nonnegative spin-only tests on at most $N$ leaves change by at most a factor $C_N$, independently of the step partition. The raw moment bounds and the actual-path centered-overlap bounds above therefore hold throughout such a segment with this factor.
  - Explanation: Transfers nonnegative spin-test estimates along admissible bounded row changes without a hierarchy-mesh loss.
- **claim-observed-block-truncation-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-truncation)): Fix an old tree with at most $N$ leaves in a block of mass $U$. Deterministic scalar coefficients may depend on all its old split values and earlier test expectations, but not on a new leaf $c$’s deeper placement. Replace $c$, after an occupied prefix of mass $v$, by an independent continuation from that prefix. The signed sum of deeper placements having this same truncated value is $v$ times that value. The telescoping difference between truncations at adjacent comparable scales $v,2v$ has total variation at most $C_Nv$. The final layer may include coincidences.
  - Explanation: Collapses placement-independent deeper rows exactly and controls adjacent cut layers, including terminal coincidences.
- **claim-boundary-covariance-baseline-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-boundary)): Let $F_c$ be a product of two factors of the form $h_j+b_jR_{cd}$, where $d$ is exterior to the block of $c$ and their low split satisfies $j=j(c,d)\in\{i,*\}$; one exterior endpoint may be conditionally projected. Let $P$ be admissible. If no overlap edge of $P$ is internal to the old block in which $c$ is allocated, the placement sum after subtracting the fresh-at-cut baseline is $o(Wx^2p_0)$, uniformly at fixed degree.
  - Explanation: Bounds the observed-block attachment correction after removing its fresh-at-cut baseline.
- **claim-admissible-test-family-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-family)): Centering an admissible test, multiplying by a projected star factor at its target, or passing to a fresh target and multiplying by one centered star edge to a summed high relative in the former target block preserves Definition <a href="#def:cavity-tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:cavity-tests">30</a> at a larger finite degree. New coefficients and attachment measures are bounded in terms of the resulting degree.
  - Explanation: Preserves the boundary structure and finite-degree control under centering, projected factors and target switches.
- **claim-finite-degree-row-expansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-row-identity)): Let $a,b$ split at $*$, with $b$ fresh, and let $P$ be admissible of degree $N$. Let $A$ and $B^{\prime}$ be the blocks at the current cut $U$ containing $a$ and $b$, respectively. Then $$\label{eq:24}\tag{24} \begin{split} \nu[R_{ab}P]={}&\Gamma(K_*)\nu[P] +b_*\sum_{c\in A,d\in B'}\gamma_{ac}\gamma_{bd} \nu[\Delta_{cd}P]\\ &+\sum_{c\in A,d\in B'} \nu[P(G_{ac}\gamma_{bd}+\gamma_{ac}G_{bd}) (K_*+b_*\Delta_{cd})] +\mathcal R_N(P). \end{split}$$ At fixed degree, uniformly over initial targets $i$ in the band of Proposition <a href="#prop:cavity-closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cavity-closure">29</a>, the remainder satisfies $$\|\mathcal R_N(P)\|\le C_NWx^2p_0\{\eta_{N,n}+(D_i/x)^{\vartheta_N}\},\qquad \eta_{N,n}\to0,\quad\vartheta_N>0,$$ where the $D_i$ term is absent without an initial factor. Consequently $\mathcal R_N(P)=o(Wx^2p_0)$ for star-only tests and along every moving initial target with $D_i=o(x)$.
  - Explanation: Expands the tested overlap into its scalar value, centered linear contribution, connected high insertions and a uniform remainder.
- **claim-projected-row-replacements** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-replacements)): After averaging the fresh partner block, $$\sum_{c\in A}\gamma_{ac}\nu[Y_cP] =\chi\nu[Y_aP]+o(Wx^2p_0).$$ In the centered part multiplying $G_{ac}$, $Y_c$ can likewise be replaced by $Y_a$, with error $o(Wx^2p_0)$. In the part multiplying $G_{bd}$, interchanging $b,d$ and summing that block gives exactly $\sum_c\gamma_{ac}\mathcal L(P\Delta_{bc},b)$. The uniform target modulus of Lemma <a href="#lem:cavity-row-identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cavity-row-identity">35</a> applies.
  - Explanation: Replaces projected variables by the distinguished target after fresh-block averaging and identifies the switched longitudinal insertion.
- **claim-fixed-depth-longitudinal-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-recursion)): Put $J_0=T\chi^2-1$. Every admissible fixed-degree test satisfies, uniformly over initial targets $i$ in the band of Proposition <a href="#prop:cavity-closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cavity-closure">29</a>, $$\|\mathcal L(P,a)\|\le Cx^2p_0\{\eta_n+(D_i/x)^\vartheta+\eta_n\|J_0\|/W^2\}, \qquad \eta_n\to0,\quad\vartheta>0.$$ The $D_i$ term is absent for star-only tests. Consequently, for such tests and along moving initial targets with $D_i=o(x)$, $$\label{eq:26}\tag{26} \mathcal L(P,a)=o\bigl(x^2p_0(1+\|J_0\|/W^2)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Iterates admissible star-factor insertions to a depth fixed before the size limit, suppressing the longitudinal correction up to its susceptibility factor.
- **claim-susceptibility-calibration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cavity-calibration)): One has $J_0=O(W^2)$. Therefore $$\label{eq:27}\tag{27} \mathcal L(P,a)=o(x^2p_0)$$ for admissible tests along moving targets with $D_i=o(x)$, and uniformly $$\|\mathcal L(P,a)\|\le Cx^2p_0\{\eta_n+(D_i/x)^\vartheta\}.$$
  - Explanation: Calibrates the susceptibility defect and removes its unresolved factor from the longitudinal test bound.
- **claim-small-scale-linear-profile** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prof-direct-profile)): Under the preceding hypotheses, every sequence has a subsequence on which, for some $\kappa$, $$\frac{r(xv)}{x}\longrightarrow\frac{v}{\kappa} \quad\text{locally uniformly for }v\in(0,\infty), \qquad 0<c\le\kappa\le C<\infty. \tag{28}\label{eq:28}$$ The bounds on $\kappa$ depend only on $T$ and the total-clock bounds. The same conclusion holds for a comparison with an omitted lower group, provided its changed paths and the constrained competitors used in the first variation are admissible.
  - Explanation: Forces subsequential locally uniform linear quantile profiles at small direct-test scales.
- **claim-mass-radius-closure-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-prof-coordinate-closure)): On each such band, $$\frac1{x^3}\int_{I_n(c,C)}D(r)^2\,dr\longrightarrow0.$$ The analogous statement holds with physical mass in place of radius. Moreover, with $$\rho_n(i)=\frac{S_i-\Gamma_n(K_i)}{x^3},$$ there is a uniform bound $\|\rho_n(i)\|\le C_{c,C}$ on the band, and $$\frac1x\int_{I_n(c,C)}\|\rho_n(r)\|\,dr\longrightarrow0.$$ The corresponding normalized mass integral also tends to zero. The uniform bound includes arbitrary moving split locations in the specified bands; along any such sequence with $D_i=o(x)$, $\rho_n(i)$ tends to zero.
  - Explanation: Controls dispersion and normalized scalar-closure defects in both coordinates, including arbitrary moving splits on the specified bands.
- **claim-scalar-derivatives-strict-curvature** (bound, [source](#stmt-prof-scalar-derivatives)): For scalar clocks with a fixed bound on total variance, positive-order spatial derivatives of every fixed order are uniformly bounded. They are absolutely continuous in scalar time, with uniformly bounded almost-everywhere time derivatives of each fixed spatial order. The function $V$ is even in $z$, $V_{zz}>0$, and $$\widehat\Gamma'(q)=T\mathbb E V_{zz}(q,Z_q)^2, \qquad \widehat\Gamma''(q)=T^2\mathbb E\bigl[ V_{zzz}(q,Z_q)^2-2\alpha_K(q)V_{zz}(q,Z_q)^3\bigr]$$ for almost every $q$. There are constants $c,C>0$, depending only on $T$ and the total-clock bound, such that $$c\le V_{zz}(0,0)\le C, \qquad c\le -V_{zzzz}(0,0)\le C.$$
  - Explanation: Provides uniform scalar derivative bounds, diffusion identities and strict bounds on the initial second and fourth spatial derivatives.
- **claim-rescaled-scalar-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prof-scalar-compactness)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#prof:direct-profile" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prof:direct-profile">39</a>, pass to a subsequence. There is a locally finite measure $\nu$ on $[0,\infty)$, with cumulative function $U(t)=\nu([0,t])$, such that the rescaled distributions of $r$ and of $K/T$ both converge locally weakly to $\nu$. For sufficiently large $t$, $U(t)$ is bounded above and below by fixed positive multiples of $t$. Further subselection gives $$\begin{gathered} g_n(t)=\frac{\Gamma_n(Txt)-xt}{x^3} \longrightarrow g(t)\quad\text{in }C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}([0,\infty)), \\ g(0)=0,\qquad g''(t)=At-BU(t)\quad\text{for almost every }t, \qquad A,B>0. \end{gathered} \tag{29}\label{eq:29}$$ The function $g$ vanishes on $\operatorname{supp}\nu$. The constants $A,B$ have positive lower bounds and finite upper bounds depending only on $T$ and the total-clock bound.
  - Explanation: Extracts a common weak limit for overlap radii and scalar clocks, together with a scalar defect vanishing on its support.
- **claim-uniform-low-clock-linearization** (bound, [source](#stmt-prof-low-clock-linearization)): For each fixed $C<\infty$, under the small direct-test hypotheses, $$\sup_{0\le K\le Cx}\|\Gamma_n(K)-K/T\|=O(x^3), \qquad \Gamma'_{n,K}(0)=T^{-1}+O(x^2).$$ Only the portion of this clock interval present in the scalar continuation is needed; for all sufficiently large $n$ it contains every displayed fixed multiple of $x$. The assertion is uniform along arbitrary admissible sequences with the stated fixed bounds. It allows an omitted mass $u_0=o(x)$ and requires no version of <a href="#eq:4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:4">[eq:4]</a> on that omitted group.
  - Explanation: Linearizes the scalar moment curve uniformly down to zero without imposing endpoint expansions on an omitted lower group.
- **claim-virtual-radius-stationarity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prof-virtual-potential)): Let $l=\inf\operatorname{supp}\nu$. On compact radius intervals $t>l$, subselection gives a monotone limit $s(t)=\lim S(xt)/x$ almost everywhere. The normalized stationarity quantity $G(xt)/x^3$ is uniformly bounded on each such interval and converges strongly in $L^1$ to a function $F$. There are two cases. If $e(x)/x^2\to\infty$, then $$s(t)=t,\qquad F(t)=-2Tg(t).$$ Otherwise, after subselection, $e(xt)/x^2\to h_1(t)>0$ and the logarithmic slopes converge locally; then $$g(s(t))=\frac{h_1(t)}T(s(t)-t),$$ and $F(t)$ has the sign of $t-s(t)$, with equality precisely when $t=s(t)$.* *If $(a_1,a_2)$ is a gap in $\operatorname{supp}\nu$ with endpoints in that support and positive cumulative mass on the gap, there is a nonnegative Lipschitz function $\mathcal H$ on $[a_1,a_2]$ with zero endpoint values such that $$\mathcal H'(t)=j(t)F(t)\quad\text{almost everywhere}, \qquad 0<c\le j(t)\le C.$$ In the bounded-strength case, $s(t)\in[a_1,a_2]$ almost everywhere on the gap.
  - Explanation: Constructs the limiting stationarity quantity and a nonnegative potential on candidate support gaps, using radius estimates even where limiting mass vanishes.
- **claim-macroscopic-scalar-variational-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prof-macro-variational)): Let $b,h$ be bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths, with $b(u)\ge b_*>0$. Then $$\lim_{n\to\infty} f_n(b,h) =\min_z\left\{f_{\rm spin}(h+bz) +\frac14\int_0^1 b(u)z(u)^2\,du\right\}.$$ The conclusion also holds for uniformly bounded paths $b_n,h_n$ converging to $b,h$ in $L^1(0,1)$, provided every actual path is admissible. No continuity of the limiting paths is required.
  - Explanation: Identifies the macroscopic hierarchical pressure with a scalar variational minimum and permits admissible varying endpoint paths.
- **claim-unique-scalar-minimizer-positive-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prof-scalar-minimizer)): The scalar variational problem has a unique minimizing distribution, denoted by $\alpha_T$, and a unique quantile up to null sets, denoted by $q_T$. There is a constant $\kappa_T>0$ such that $$\alpha_T(s)=\kappa_Ts+o(s)\quad(s\downarrow0).$$ Its quantile is continuous at every mass in $(0,1)$ and satisfies $c_Tu\le q_T(u)\le C_Tu$ there.
  - Explanation: Identifies the unique scalar minimizing distribution and quantile, proves positive initial density and obtains global comparison with mass.
- **claim-macroscopic-quantile-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prof-macro-profile)): Consider minimizers in <a href="#eq:6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:6">[eq:6]</a> satisfying the endpoint and constraint hypotheses <a href="#eq:3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:3">[eq:3]</a>–<a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a>. Suppose $x\ge c>0$, $e(x)/x^2\ge c>0$, and $d^2\le T-c$ along the sequence. Then $$r_n\longrightarrow q_T\quad\text{in }L^1(0,1) \quad\text{and locally uniformly on }(0,1).$$ An omitted mass $u_0=o(a)$ and its specified admissible changed paths are allowed, provided the changed paths of the minimizing sequence and the smooth competitors used in the proof are admissible. In particular all macroscopic quantile limits are bounded above and below by fixed positive multiples of mass.
  - Explanation: Makes constrained minimizing quantiles converge to the scalar Parisi quantile at macroscopic positive strengths.
- **claim-child-comparison-data-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-reg-child-data)): Suppose a parent comparison has data <a href="#eq:3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:3">[eq:3]</a>–<a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a> and an optimizing quantile with an outer regular region above $x$, where $$A(\lambda)\le x\le1,\qquad e(x)=o(x^2).$$ Fix $c_0>0$, independent of $n$, and set $$d'=c_0x,\qquad E'=\min\bigl\{n^{-1/2}d'^2, n^{-100\delta}e(x)x^3\bigr\},\qquad a'=\frac{\sqrt n E'}{d'}.$$ Then, for all sufficiently large $n$, the child data satisfy <a href="#eq:3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:3">[eq:3]</a>, with the same $m$, and $$E'\ge n^{\delta/4}E,\qquad a'\ge a.$$ The changed parent path, used as the child’s bare endpoint, satisfies <a href="#eq:4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:4">[eq:4]</a> at all its required test scales. An omitted mass interval of length $u_0=o(a)$ remains $o(a')$.
  - Explanation: Builds valid child parameters with a polynomially larger error budget while preserving endpoint expansions and omitted-mass compatibility.
- **claim-admissible-parent-child-movement** (bound, [source](#stmt-reg-admissible-movement)): Let $r$ minimize the parent objective at a fixed positive strength, and let a child comparison act on $J\supseteq I$. Denote its final profile by $$\mathcal D(z)=d'^2 f(z/d'),$$ and its final minimizing quantile by $z$. Suppose its error bound is successful, so the admissible doubly changed path $$Q=\bigl(P_{r,b}-\mathcal D(z)\mathbf1_J, P_{r,h}+\mathcal D(z)z\mathbf1_J\bigr)$$ satisfies $$f_n(Q)+\frac14\int_J\mathcal D(z)z^2\,du \le f_n(P_r)+\mathrm{err}.$$ Let $\widehat r$ be a feasible parent trial, equal to $r$ outside the common working region, and suppose at every moved point $$\widehat r=r+\omega(z-r),\qquad 0\le\omega<\frac{\mathcal D(z)}{e(r)+\mathcal D(z)}.$$ If its changed path $P_{\widehat r}$ is admissible, then $$\int_I e(r)\omega(z-r)^2\,du\le4\,\mathrm{err}.$$ This conclusion does not require an admissible bare parent endpoint or a parent error bound.
  - Explanation: Bounds a feasible parent displacement toward the child by the child error, evaluating only admissible changed paths.
- **claim-feasible-separation-barriers** (construction, [source](#stmt-reg-barriers)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#reg:child-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:child-data">49</a>, let $y\gtrsim x$. Suppose, on an interval of mass length comparable to $y$ at masses comparable to $y$, the parent and child quantiles are separated by two fixed thresholds whose distance is comparable to $y$. Assume $r\le Cy$ there, but impose no upper bound on $z/y$. There is a feasible parent movement as in Lemma <a href="#reg:admissible-movement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:admissible-movement">50</a> for which $$\int e(r)\omega(z-r)^2\,du \ge c\min\{e(y),\mathcal D(y)\}y^3.$$ Constants may depend on the separation thresholds and on the fixed comparability constants.
  - Explanation: Constructs quantile movements preserving both derivative constraints and converting threshold separation into a definite comparison gain.
- **claim-successful-child-profile-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-reg-successful-child)): In Lemma <a href="#reg:child-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:child-data">49</a>, suppose the child’s final error is at most $n^{20\delta}E'$. If $x\to0$, every sequence has a subsequence on which the parent quantile satisfies <a href="#eq:28" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:28">[eq:28]</a>, with the bounds on $\kappa$ stated there. If $x$ stays bounded below by a positive constant, it converges locally uniformly on $(0,1)$ to the Parisi quantile $q_T$.
  - Explanation: Uses a successful child error bound to transfer small-scale or macroscopic rigidity to the parent.
- **claim-finite-propagation-global-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-reg-finite-propagation)): For every sequence of comparison families in Definition <a href="#reg:families" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:families">48</a> with data <a href="#eq:3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:3">[eq:3]</a>–<a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a>, there are fixed positive constants $c<C$ such that every minimizing quantile at a positive strength $0<\lambda\le d^2$ satisfies $$cu\le r(u)\le Cu\qquad \left(n^\delta\frac{\sqrt n E}{\sqrt\lambda}\le u\le1\right)$$ for all sufficiently large $n$ along that sequence. For ordinary admissible bare endpoints, the entire comparison satisfies $$0\le\mathcal V(\lambda)-f_n(b_0,h_0) \le n^{20\delta}E, \qquad 0\le\lambda\le d^2.$$ For a fixed strength family only the profile assertion is made; its proof uses error estimates exclusively for admissible child endpoints.
  - Explanation: Removes the outer-regularity assumption through a fixed descendant chain and yields the full comparison error only for ordinary admissible endpoints.
- **claim-field-wall-profile-uniformity** (bound, [source](#stmt-reg-wall-uniformity)): Let the parent have data <a href="#eq:3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:3">[eq:3]</a>–<a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a> and work on $[s,1]$, with $s=o(a)$, at final strength $e(q)=d^2f(q/d)$ and lower wall $0<r_{\min}\le u_*/M(s)$. Suppose $d=o(1)$ and $d^2=o(\varepsilon)$. For $0<\varepsilon\le T$ and $0\le\theta\le1$, put $$c_L=\frac12\min(\varepsilon,T-\varepsilon),\qquad r_0=\frac{d^2r_{\min}}{2\varepsilon},$$ and take increasing $0\le\varphi\le1$ on $[0,s]$. The bare algebraic coefficients are $$b_0=T-(\varepsilon+\theta c_L)\mathbf1_{u<s},\qquad h_0=\theta c_Lr_0\varphi(u)\mathbf1_{u<s}.$$ The profile conclusion of Proposition <a href="#reg:finite-propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:finite-propagation">53</a> holds uniformly along arbitrary sequences of these wall parameters, including $\theta\to0$ and $T-\varepsilon\to0$.
  - Explanation: Preserves the profile assertion along arbitrary wall-parameter sequences through fixed covariance-margin alternatives.
- **claim-admissible-path-chord-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-fl-chord)): Suppose $(b,h)$ and $(b+a,h-c)$ are bounded admissible paths, $a\ge0$, and $c=0$ wherever $a=0$. If $c^2/a$ is integrable, with the quotient set to zero where $a=c=0$, then $$f_n(b+a,h-c)\le f_n(b,h)+\frac14\int_0^1\frac{c(u)^2}{a(u)}\,du.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds pressure between admissible endpoint paths by completing the square in the overlap mean.
- **claim-unswept-wall-admissibility-profile** (bound, [source](#stmt-fl-wall-profile)): For all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly in $\varepsilon\in[\varepsilon_0,T)$ and $\theta\in[0,1]$, every path in the definition of $J_\theta$ is admissible. Its minimizing high quantiles have the regular outer profile <a href="#eq:8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:8">[eq:8]</a>; in particular they are regular above scale $d$. In using this profile assertion, no pressure is evaluated at the bare low-field path with its high comparison removed.
  - Explanation: Establishes admissibility and high-quantile regularity for the wall functional without evaluating its inadmissible bare low-field coefficients.
- **claim-low-group-mean-suppression** (bound, [source](#stmt-fl-low-mean)): For every minimizing changed path in Lemma <a href="#fl:wall-profile" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fl:wall-profile">56</a>, uniformly over its low-group splits, $$S_u=\frac{K_u}{T}+O(d^3),\qquad S_u\le\frac{C d^3}{\varepsilon}=:a_L, \qquad K_u=h_u+b_uS_u,\quad 0\le u<s. \tag{31}\label{eq:31}$$ The constants are uniform in the wall parameters.
  - Explanation: Combines omitted-group cavity control and scalar linearization to suppress the mean overlap uniformly on the low group.
- **claim-wall-sweep-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-fl-wall-cost)): With the preceding choices, uniformly in $\varepsilon_0\le\varepsilon<T$, $$\begin{split} J_1^*-J_0^* \le C\left[ c_Ls(a_L^2+r_0^2) +\frac{a_L(\log n)^3}{nr_0s}+n^{-10}\right]. \end{split}$$ Consequently, $$p(\varepsilon+q)-p(\varepsilon) \le n^{20\delta}E +C\left[s\frac{d^6}{\varepsilon_0} +\frac{d}{nsr_{\min}}(\log n)^3+n^{-10}\right].$$
  - Explanation: Controls the low-field sweep and the resulting pressure increments through pinned moments, trace estimates and a chord comparison.
- **claim-positive-laplace-window** (bound, [source](#stmt-fl-laplace-window)): For every fixed $T>1$ and $0<m<1/300$, there exist $c_{T,m}>0$, $C_{T,m}<\infty$, and $n_0(T,m)$ such that $$c_{T,m}n^{20m}\le \log\mathbb E\exp\{s(F_n^c-\mathbb EF_n^c)\} \le C_{T,m}n^{100m},\qquad s=n^{-1/6+5m},$$ for all $n\ge n_0(T,m)$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the positive centered log-Laplace transform at the prescribed scale between diverging polynomial windows.
- **claim-exponential-log-concave-augmentation** (construction, [source](#stmt-fl-augmentation)): Let $\mathcal E$ be an independent exponential variable of rate one. Then $G_n=F_n^c+\mathcal E$ has a log-concave density. For $0\le t<1$, $$\log\mathbb E e^{t(G_n-\mathbb EG_n)} =K_n(t)-t-\log(1-t),\qquad \operatorname{Var}G_n=\operatorname{Var}F_n^c+1.$$
  - Explanation: Adds an independent exponential variable to obtain a log-concave density and exact transform and variance corrections.
- **claim-log-concave-variance-tail-density-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-fl-one-dimensional)): Let $X$ be centered with a log-concave density and $\sigma^2=\mathbb E X^2>0$. There are universal positive constants $c,C$ such that $$\begin{split} &\mathbb P\{\|X\|\ge t\sigma\}\le2e^{-ct}\quad(t\ge0),\\ &\log\mathbb E e^{tX}\le C t^2\sigma^2 \quad(0\le t\sigma\le c),\\ &\mathbb P\{X\ge c\sigma\}\ge c,\qquad \log\mathbb E e^{tX}\ge ct\sigma+\log c\quad(t\ge0),\\ &\sup_x f_X(x)\le C/\sigma. \end{split}$$ The lower log-moment inequality is understood also when the moment is infinite.
  - Explanation: Controls tails, positive exponential moments, positive-side mass and density height for a centered log-concave variable.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-found-replica",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K_\\theta(u;\\sigma,\\tau)$ be an affine family of cumulative Gaussian covariance kernels of a finite hierarchy. Assume that the independent increment covariances are positive semidefinite throughout the parameter interval. Let $V=\\partial_\\theta K_\\theta$ and let $P$ be a fixed bounded spin test on a fixed observed split tree. Then $$\\frac{d}{d\\theta}\\nu_\\theta[P]\n      =\\frac12\\sum_{c,d}\\nu_\\theta[P V_{cd}].\n \\tag{2}\\label{eq:2}$$ The signed sum includes coincidences with old labels and between $c,d$. For distinct labels, $V_{cd}$ is evaluated at their common revelation; on the diagonal it is evaluated at the terminal covariance. The exact weights are the real-power weights described in the proof. A constant diagonal contribution, independent of the summation label, sums to zero. Conditional means in $P$ can be represented by independent additional replicas at their specified prefixes before this formula is applied.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-attachment",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an enclosing block of mass $a\\in[0,1]$ containing $r\\geq1$ observed leaves. After grouping placements which induce the same extended tree, one free summation label has the following signed attachment measure:*\n\n1.  *weight $+1$ for coincidence with each observed leaf;*\n\n2.  *measure $-dm$ on every occupied open edge, parameterized by its increasing mass coordinate;*\n\n3.  *weight $(1-d_v)u_v$ at an observed fork of mass $u_v$ with $d_v$ occupied children, including an initial fork if present.*\n\n*The total signed mass is $a$ and the total variation is $2r-a\\leq2r$. For an empty block the first label has weight $a$. Consequently, adjoining $k$ labels to a tree with $r\\geq1$ old labels has total variation at most $$2^k r(r+1)\\cdots(r+k-1).$$ Adjoining $k\\geq1$ labels in a previously empty block has variation at most $a\\,2^{k-1}(k-1)!$. These bounds are independent of the mass partition. A free row whose summand is independent of the detailed attachment inside a block of mass $u$ collapses exactly to $u$ times that summand.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-attachment",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an enclosing block of mass $a\\in[0,1]$ containing $r\\geq1$ observed leaves. These bounds are independent of the mass partition. A free row whose summand is independent of the detailed attachment inside a block of mass $u$ collapses exactly to $u$ times that summand.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-split-density",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite observed tree with at most $q$ leaves and allocate at most $k$ new labels. Apart from coincidences and atoms at specified old forks or cuts, the split mass of any specified pair involving a new label has a Lebesgue density with total variation bounded by a constant $C_{q,k}$. The same statement holds when some old fork masses are themselves integrated against a measure of bounded density, with the resulting bound multiplied by that density bound. These assertions concern the signed attachment measure and its variation, not the size of a spin observable at a pointwise split level.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-taylor",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:found-replica\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:found-replica\">6</a>, suppose $P$ uses at most $q$ labels and $|V(u;\\sigma,\\tau)|\\leq M$. For each fixed integer $j\\geq1$ there is $C_{q,j}<\\infty$, independent of the number of covariance steps, such that $$\\left|\\frac{d^j}{d\\theta^j}\\nu_\\theta[P]\\right|\n       \\leq C_{q,j}M^j\\|P\\|_\\infty.$$ More precisely the derivative is an integral over a signed measure of extended trees with uniformly bounded total variation, of expectations of $P$ times $j$ covariance insertions, with the factor $2^{-j}$. Hölder’s inequality can therefore be applied to these integrands under their genuine probability laws. If $P\\geq0$, then $$\\left|\\frac{d}{d\\theta}\\nu_\\theta[P]\\right|\n        \\leq C_{q,1}M\\nu_\\theta[P],$$ and hence $$e^{-C_{q,1}M|\\theta-\\theta'|}\\nu_{\\theta'}[P]\n \\leq\\nu_\\theta[P]\n \\leq e^{C_{q,1}M|\\theta-\\theta'|}\\nu_{\\theta'}[P].$$ The assertions require that the test, including any scalar centers in it, be fixed during the interpolation. For parameter-dependent tests the ordinary product-rule derivatives must also be included.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-cut-truncation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an old tree with at most $q$ leaves in a block of mass $U$, and a free row label $r$ in that block. Let $U\\leq v\\leq w\\leq\\min(2v,1)$. The difference of its signed row tests truncated at $w$ and at $v$ can, after exact grouping of the deeper placements, be written as a signed combination of pairs of planted laws with total coefficient variation at most $C_qv$. In each pair the old-label tree is the same, and only the coupling of $r$ to one occupied descendant subtree after the revelation at $v$ differs. One may take $C_q=12q$.*\n\n*The statement remains true when the row is multiplied by a bounded coefficient depending on the fixed old topology, multiplied into the bound by its supremum. For the exact collapse of deeper placements this coefficient, and all scalar centers in the row test, must be independent of $r$’s detailed attachment beyond the truncation cut. Allocation-order symmetry carries the full topology coefficient with it; it does not establish the independence required for a free-row collapse. At each recursion generation, freeze the previously generated topology, its coefficients, and earlier scalar centers before introducing fresh slots. A current coefficient depending on the row’s deeper placement must remain explicit until eliminated or estimated. Only after this row calculation may the enlarged topology be frozen for the next generation.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-coupling",
        "exact_quote": "Consider either pair of laws in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:found-cut-truncation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:found-cut-truncation\">12</a>. Let $\\mathcal G$ be the sigma-field generated by their complete common prefix through the prescribed revelation at $v$, the realizations of all old branches outside the affected descendant subtree after $v$, and the deterministic old topology. No information from the continuation of $r$ or from the affected old subtree after $v$ is included in $\\mathcal G$. Let $F$ be a function of $r$ and this exterior information, and let $P$ be a function of the old subtree and the exterior information. The two laws have the same conditional marginal law of $F$ given $\\mathcal G$ and the same conditional marginal law of $P$ given $\\mathcal G$. Write their common conditional means as $\\bar F,\\bar P$. Their difference therefore obeys $$(\\mathbf E_1-\\mathbf E_0)[FP]\n   =(\\mathbf E_1-\\mathbf E_0)[(F-\\bar F)(P-\\bar P)].$$ If $p^{-1}+p'^{-1}=1$, its absolute value is at most $$\\sum_{i=0}^1\\|F-\\bar F\\|_{L^p(\\mathbf P_i)}\n                  \\|P-\\bar P\\|_{L^{p'}(\\mathbf P_i)}.$$ Each centered norm is at most the corresponding norm of the difference from an independent resampling with the same conditional marginal given $\\mathcal G$. Consequently uniform resampling bounds $A_v,B_v$ for these two factors give a bound $2C_qvA_vB_v$ for the full cut layer.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-boundary-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a cut $U$ and its old descendant blocks. Let $P$ be a polynomial of fixed degree in overlaps between different blocks, centered by fixed scalars, and in products of such overlaps with fresh auxiliary leaves split at specified cuts. Assume that each overlap involving an affected old subtree has its other endpoint outside that subtree. Bounded coefficients may depend on the entire fixed old topology. All scalar centers may be expectations on an earlier tree, provided they are held fixed during the current row interpolation.*\n\n*On resampling the affected subtree while retaining its prefix, only the overlap factors crossing its boundary vary. If each such factor has a resampling-difference bound in the required finite $L^p$ norm, and the other factors have their required finite moment bounds, then $P$ has the bound obtained by the product rule, with a constant depending only on its degree, number of monomials, and coefficient bound. In particular this property is preserved by multiplying the current target test by centered overlaps to fresh auxiliary leaves, and by introducing a fresh target block joined to an earlier target by one overlap. Earlier target blocks may have several incident boundary overlaps.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-found-limits",
        "exact_quote": "The preceding construction and estimates extend to bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths $b,h$ by step approximation. The limit is independent of the approximations, provided the terminal clocks converge and the before/after clocks at every specified cut converge to the specified values. Planted expectations of bounded spin tests on a fixed finite split tree with cuts before or after jumps, fixed-order source derivatives, the signed calculus, and the mass-integrated pressure and moment formulas have these limits. The susceptibility inequality holds on the continuous parts, with nonnegative total increments of $S$ at jumps. For intermediate cuts and the separate clock contributions inside a filled jump, use approximations retaining the prescribed joint filling; no independence from the choice of that filling is asserted. All constants in the finite-tree variation and Taylor bounds are unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-envelope",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $n$, the class <a href=\"#eq:5\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:5\">[eq:5]</a> is nonempty and compact in the uniform topology of inverse quantiles. The minimum in <a href=\"#eq:6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:6\">[eq:6]</a> is attained. For an admissible sweep, $\\mathcal M_\\lambda$ is locally Lipschitz and nondecreasing in $\\lambda$, equals the endpoint pressure at $\\lambda=0$, and is an upper bound for that pressure. At almost every positive strength its upper derivative per unit $\\log\\lambda$ is bounded, at any minimizer at that strength, by $$\\frac14\\int_{u_0}^1 e(r(u))\n           \\bigl[D_u^2+(S_u-r(u))^2\\bigr]\\,du.\n \\tag{7}\\label{eq:7}$$ All moments in this formula are for the changed clocks in <a href=\"#eq:6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:6\">[eq:6]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "At a minimizer there is a continuous absolutely continuous potential $H$ such that $H'=JG$ for a strictly positive factor $J$, $\\alpha'=M(\\alpha)$ on $\\{H<0\\}$, and $\\alpha'=k$ on $\\{H>0\\}$. On a band where $\\alpha+u_*$ varies by a bounded factor, $J$ has bounded distortion after normalization at one endpoint. On the contact set $\\{H=0\\}$, almost everywhere, $$G=0,\\qquad S'\\lesssim n^{o(1)},\\qquad S\\le r.\n \\tag{9}\\label{eq:9}$$ On an open cap component $\\{H<0\\}$ of radius length $\\ell$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\Delta S\\lesssim n^{o(1)}\n        (\\ell+r_{\\min}\\mathbf1_{\\mathrm{initial}}),\n \\qquad S\\le r+\\ell,\\\\\n |S-r|^2\\lesssim n^{o(1)}\n   \\left[D^2+\\ell^2+r_{\\min}^2\\mathbf1_{\\mathrm{initial}}\\right].\n \\end{gathered}\n \\tag{10}\\label{eq:10}$$ Here $\\Delta S$ is the increase through the interior of the component. On contact, $(S-r)^2\\lesssim D^2$. Every cap has $\\ell\\lesssim n^{-\\delta}L\\log n$; if it is contained in a comparable mass band $u\\asymp v\\gg u_*$, then $v\\ell\\lesssim n^{-\\delta}E/d^3$. A noninitial cap on which $p=0$ throughout satisfies $|S-r|\\le\\ell$. The total floor mass is at most $n^{-\\delta}u_*$.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-projection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a vector determined by outside branches and by data at or before their fork with a branch $b$. Conditional on this data, the future of $b$ has its usual planted law. Suppose a field-clock interval after the fork has length $D_h$, with mass at least $v_->0$ throughout. For even fixed $p\\ge2$, the part of $V\\cdot y_b$ left after projection at the end of the interval satisfies $$\\|V\\cdot(y_b-X_{b,\\mathrm{end}})\\|_p\n \\le C_p(\\log n)^2\\frac{\\|V\\|_p}{v_-\\sqrt{nD_h}}.\n \\tag{13}\\label{eq:13}$$ Here the masses and clock lengths used in the estimate are bounded below by inverse powers of $n$. Differences of two later projections satisfy the same estimate up to a constant.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-projection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a vector determined by outside branches and by data at or before their fork with a branch $b$.         "anchor": "stmt-comp-resampling",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose two leaves share only through a mass at most a fixed multiple of $x_1$, where $x_1\\ge x$.         "anchor": "stmt-comp-good-split",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a regular band of mass and radius of order $v\\ge Cx$ contains a mass interval of length comparable to $v$ on which $S\\asymp v$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-direct-moments",
        "exact_quote": "On every regular outer dyad of mass $u\\asymp v$ beyond a sufficiently large fixed multiple of $x$, for every sufficiently small fixed $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\left(\\operatorname{avg}_{u\\asymp v}D_u^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\lesssim_\\epsilon n^\\epsilon\\left[\n (ne(v)v)^{-1/3}+(ne(v)v^2)^{-1/2}\n +(n^{-\\delta}E/d^3)^{1/2}+n^{\\delta/2}u_*\\right].\n \\tag{18}\\label{eq:18}$$ Fixed enlargements of a dyad are permitted in this assertion.*\n\n\n*For a small direct test impose additionally $e(x)\\gtrsim x^2$, and set $W=\\max(x,\\sqrt{e(x)})$. Then $W\\lesssim d$ and $e(v)\\gtrsim W^2$ for $v=O(W)$. On regular dyads of scale at least a fixed multiple of $W$, including comparable dyads at $W$ that lie in the regular region, the right side of <a href=\"#eq:18\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:18\">[eq:18]</a> is polynomially smaller than $x^2/W$. Between $Cx$ and $O(W)$ it is polynomially smaller than $x^2/(W^2v)^{1/3}$. Moreover $$\\int_0^{CW}D_u\\,du=o(x^2),\\qquad D=o(x).\n \\tag{19}\\label{eq:19}$$ The integral is truncated at mass $1$, and $C$ may be any fixed constant. The second assertion means normalized mean-square convergence on every compact band $r\\le Cx$, $cx\\le u\\le Cx$, both in mass and in radius: $$x^{-3}\\int_{\\{r\\le Cx,\\,cx\\le u\\le Cx\\}}D^2\\,du\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n x^{-3}\\int_{\\{r\\le Cx,\\,cx\\le\\alpha(r)\\le Cx\\}}D^2\\,dr\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ If $W=o(1)$, there is a split $*$ in a regular band of scale $W$ with $S_*\\asymp W$, satisfying <a href=\"#eq:17\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:17\">[eq:17]</a>, and with $D_*$ polynomially smaller than $x^2/W$. It can be chosen above any fixed multiple of $x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-conditional-error",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda\\ge n^{-10}$ and $A=A_\\lambda=n^\\delta\\sqrt n E/\\sqrt\\lambda$. If $A=o(1)$, assume that a minimizer has a regular outer region above $A$. Then its integral <a href=\"#eq:7\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:7\">[eq:7]</a> is at most $n^{10\\delta}E$ for all sufficiently large $n$. If $A$ stays bounded below by a positive constant, the same conclusion holds without a regularity hypothesis.*\n\n*Consequently, if these hypotheses hold along an admissible sweep from zero to any strength at most $d^2$, then $0\\le\\mathcal M_\\lambda-f_n(b_0,h_0)\\le n^{20\\delta}E$. The fixed-strength assertions apply also to the wall variant, whereas this last conclusion requires an admissible sweep.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-unit-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a group at comparable masses $u\\asymp v$ of total mass at most $Cv$, and suppose the radii and the raw overlap moments in that group are bounded at scale $y_0$. Away from the floor, take as units complete cap components with comparable endpoint masses, and mass elements of the contact set. Let $e_g$ be a lower bound on $e$ in the group. If a cap has no initial-wall loss, then within each unit $$\\operatorname{avg}_{\\mathrm{unit}}\n       \\mathbb E\\operatorname{tr}H_u^2\n \\lesssim\\frac{n^{o(1)}}{ne_g}.\n \\tag{20}\\label{eq:20}$$ On contact this holds pointwise almost everywhere. In particular the unit averages of $\\mathbb E\\operatorname{tr}C_u^2$ and $\\mathbb EW_u$ are at most $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv^2)$ and $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv)$, respectively.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-unit-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a group at comparable masses $u\\asymp v$ of total mass at most $Cv$, and suppose the radii and the raw overlap moments in that group are bounded at scale $y_0$. Away from the floor, take as units complete cap components with comparable endpoint masses, and mass elements of the contact set. Let $e_g$ be a lower bound on $e$ in the group. If a cap has no initial-wall loss, then within each unit $$\\operatorname{avg}_{\\mathrm{unit}}\n       \\mathbb E\\operatorname{tr}H_u^2\n \\lesssim\\frac{n^{o(1)}}{ne_g}.\n \\tag{20}\\label{eq:20}$$ On contact this holds pointwise almost everywhere. In particular the unit averages of $\\mathbb E\\operatorname{tr}C_u^2$ and $\\mathbb EW_u$ are at most $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv^2)$ and $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv)$, respectively.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Consider a group at comparable masses $u\\asymp v$ of total mass at most $Cv$, and suppose the radii and the raw overlap moments in that group are bounded at scale $y_0$.       "id": "claim-whole-unit-trace-bounds",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-bracket",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#comp:unit-trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:unit-trace\">26</a>, let $\\ell_0$ be a common polynomial-scale upper bound for cap widths, including a possible wall term when it is covered by the trace estimate. Suppose on the group, for every fixed $p$, the raw bounds are $\\|X\\|_p\\le C_p\\sqrt{y_0}$ and $\\|R\\|_p\\le C_py_0$. Then, up to a factor $n^{O(\\epsilon')}$ for any fixed $\\epsilon'>0$, and an edge error $O(k_0n^{-10})$, $$\\int_{\\mathrm{group}}D_u^2\\,du\n \\lesssim v\\left[\n       (y_0/k_0)^2+y_0\\ell_0+\\ell_0^2\n              +\\frac{k_0+1}{ne_gv^2}\\right],\n \\qquad 1\\le k_0\\le n^2.\n \\tag{21}\\label{eq:21}$$ In particular the first and last variable terms may be replaced by $C[(y_0/(ne_gv^2))^{2/3}+1/(ne_gv^2)]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-bracket",
          "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#comp:unit-trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:unit-trace\">26</a>, let $\\ell_0$ be a common polynomial-scale upper bound for cap widths, including a possible wall term when it is covered by the trace estimate. Suppose on the group, for every fixed $p$, the raw bounds are $\\|X\\|_p\\le C_p\\sqrt{y_0}$ and $\\|R\\|_p\\le C_py_0$. Then, up to a factor $n^{O(\\epsilon')}$ for any fixed $\\epsilon'>0$, and an edge error $O(k_0n^{-10})$, $$\\int_{\\mathrm{group}}D_u^2\\,du\n \\lesssim v\\left[\n       (y_0/k_0)^2+y_0\\ell_0+\\ell_0^2\n              +\\frac{k_0+1}{ne_gv^2}\\right],\n \\qquad 1\\le k_0\\le n^2.\n \\tag{21}\\label{eq:21}$$ In particular the first and last variable terms may be replaced by $C[(y_0/(ne_gv^2))^{2/3}+1/(ne_gv^2)]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<c<C<\\infty$. At a target cut $i$ satisfying $r_i\\le Cx$ and $cx\\le u_i\\le Cx$, along every sequence with $D_i=o(x)$, $$\\label{eq:22}\\tag{22}\n                         S_i=\\Gamma(K_i)+o(x^3).$$ The bound is $O(x^3)$, uniformly over these targets, if $D_i=O(x)$. More precisely, for a fixed direct-test sequence there are $\\eta_n\\to0$, $C_0<\\infty$, and $\\vartheta>0$, independent of the target in this band, such that $$\\frac{|S_i-\\Gamma(K_i)|}{x^3}\n       \\le C_0\\{\\eta_n+(D_i/x)^\\vartheta\\}.$$ When $W=O(x)$, the $O(x^3)$ assertion also holds at every omitted lower cut, without a lower bound on its mass and without <a href=\"#eq:4\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:4\">[eq:4]</a> at that cut.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-truncation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an old tree with at most $N$ leaves in a block of mass $U$. Deterministic scalar coefficients may depend on all its old split values and earlier test expectations, but not on a new leaf $c$’s deeper placement. Replace $c$, after an occupied prefix of mass $v$, by an independent continuation from that prefix. The signed sum of deeper placements having this same truncated value is $v$ times that value. The telescoping difference between truncations at adjacent comparable scales $v,2v$ has total variation at most $C_Nv$. The final layer may include coincidences.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $a,b$ split at $*$, with $b$ fresh, and let $P$ be admissible of degree $N$. Then $$\\label{eq:24}\\tag{24}\n \\begin{split}\n \\nu[R_{ab}P]={}&\\Gamma(K_*)\\nu[P]\n  +b_*\\sum_{c\\in A,d\\in B'}\\gamma_{ac}\\gamma_{bd}\n                                             \\nu[\\Delta_{cd}P]\\\\\n &+\\sum_{c\\in A,d\\in B'}\n   \\nu[P(G_{ac}\\gamma_{bd}+\\gamma_{ac}G_{bd})\n                                     (K_*+b_*\\Delta_{cd})]\n   +\\mathcal R_N(P).\n \\end{split}$$ At fixed degree, uniformly over initial targets $i$ in the band of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cavity-closure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cavity-closure\">29</a>, the remainder satisfies $$|\\mathcal R_N(P)|\\le\n C_NWx^2p_0\\{\\eta_{N,n}+(D_i/x)^{\\vartheta_N}\\},\\qquad\n       \\eta_{N,n}\\to0,\\quad\\vartheta_N>0,$$ where the $D_i$ term is absent without an initial factor. Consequently $\\mathcal R_N(P)=o(Wx^2p_0)$ for star-only tests and along every moving initial target with $D_i=o(x)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Put $J_0=T\\chi^2-1$.         "anchor": "stmt-prof-coordinate-closure",
        "exact_quote": "On each such band, $$\\frac1{x^3}\\int_{I_n(c,C)}D(r)^2\\,dr\\longrightarrow0.$$ The analogous statement holds with physical mass in place of radius.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prof-scalar-derivatives",
        "exact_quote": "For scalar clocks with a fixed bound on total variance, positive-order spatial derivatives of every fixed order are uniformly bounded. They are absolutely continuous in scalar time, with uniformly bounded almost-everywhere time derivatives of each fixed spatial order. The function $V$ is even in $z$, $V_{zz}>0$, and $$\\widehat\\Gamma'(q)=T\\mathbb E V_{zz}(q,Z_q)^2,\n \\qquad\n \\widehat\\Gamma''(q)=T^2\\mathbb E\\bigl[\n V_{zzz}(q,Z_q)^2-2\\alpha_K(q)V_{zz}(q,Z_q)^3\\bigr]$$ for almost every $q$. There are constants $c,C>0$, depending only on $T$ and the total-clock bound, such that $$c\\le V_{zz}(0,0)\\le C,\n \\qquad c\\le -V_{zzzz}(0,0)\\le C.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prof-scalar-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#prof:direct-profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:direct-profile\">39</a>, pass to a subsequence. There is a locally finite measure $\\nu$ on $[0,\\infty)$, with cumulative function $U(t)=\\nu([0,t])$, such that the rescaled distributions of $r$ and of $K/T$ both converge locally weakly to $\\nu$. For sufficiently large $t$, $U(t)$ is bounded above and below by fixed positive multiples of $t$. Further subselection gives $$\\begin{gathered}\n g_n(t)=\\frac{\\Gamma_n(Txt)-xt}{x^3}\n       \\longrightarrow g(t)\\quad\\text{in }C^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}([0,\\infty)),\n \\\\\n g(0)=0,\\qquad g''(t)=At-BU(t)\\quad\\text{for almost every }t,\n \\qquad A,B>0.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\tag{29}\\label{eq:29}$$ The function $g$ vanishes on $\\operatorname{supp}\\nu$. The constants $A,B$ have positive lower bounds and finite upper bounds depending only on $T$ and the total-clock bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-prof-low-clock-linearization",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed $C<\\infty$, under the small direct-test hypotheses, $$\\sup_{0\\le K\\le Cx}|\\Gamma_n(K)-K/T|=O(x^3),\n \\qquad \\Gamma'_{n,K}(0)=T^{-1}+O(x^2).$$ Only the portion of this clock interval present in the scalar continuation is needed; for all sufficiently large $n$ it contains every displayed fixed multiple of $x$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prof-virtual-potential",
        "exact_quote": "Let $l=\\inf\\operatorname{supp}\\nu$. On compact radius intervals $t>l$, subselection gives a monotone limit $s(t)=\\lim S(xt)/x$ almost everywhere. The normalized stationarity quantity $G(xt)/x^3$ is uniformly bounded on each such interval and converges strongly in $L^1$ to a function $F$. There are two cases. If $e(x)/x^2\\to\\infty$, then $$s(t)=t,\\qquad F(t)=-2Tg(t).$$ Otherwise, after subselection, $e(xt)/x^2\\to h_1(t)>0$ and the logarithmic slopes converge locally; then $$g(s(t))=\\frac{h_1(t)}T(s(t)-t),$$ and $F(t)$ has the sign of $t-s(t)$, with equality precisely when $t=s(t)$.*\n\n*If $(a_1,a_2)$ is a gap in $\\operatorname{supp}\\nu$ with endpoints in that support and positive cumulative mass on the gap, there is a nonnegative Lipschitz function $\\mathcal H$ on $[a_1,a_2]$ with zero endpoint values such that $$\\mathcal H'(t)=j(t)F(t)\\quad\\text{almost everywhere},\n \\qquad 0<c\\le j(t)\\le C.$$ In the bounded-strength case, $s(t)\\in[a_1,a_2]$ almost everywhere on the gap.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prof-virtual-potential",

          "source_id": "PM-217.2"

        "exact_quote": "Let $b,h$ be bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths, with $b(u)\\ge b_*>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prof-scalar-minimizer",
        "exact_quote": "The scalar variational problem has a unique minimizing distribution, denoted by $\\alpha_T$, and a unique quantile up to null sets, denoted by $q_T$.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-reg-admissible-movement",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r$ minimize the parent objective at a fixed positive strength, and let a child comparison act on $J\\supseteq I$. Denote its final profile by $$\\mathcal D(z)=d'^2 f(z/d'),$$ and its final minimizing quantile by $z$. Suppose its error bound is successful, so the admissible doubly changed path $$Q=\\bigl(P_{r,b}-\\mathcal D(z)\\mathbf1_J,\n              P_{r,h}+\\mathcal D(z)z\\mathbf1_J\\bigr)$$ satisfies $$f_n(Q)+\\frac14\\int_J\\mathcal D(z)z^2\\,du\n             \\le f_n(P_r)+\\mathrm{err}.$$ Let $\\widehat r$ be a feasible parent trial, equal to $r$ outside the common working region, and suppose at every moved point $$\\widehat r=r+\\omega(z-r),\\qquad\n 0\\le\\omega<\\frac{\\mathcal D(z)}{e(r)+\\mathcal D(z)}.$$ If its changed path $P_{\\widehat r}$ is admissible, then $$\\int_I e(r)\\omega(z-r)^2\\,du\\le4\\,\\mathrm{err}.$$ This conclusion does not require an admissible bare parent endpoint or a parent error bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-reg-admissible-movement",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r$ minimize the parent objective at a fixed positive strength, and let a child comparison act on $J\\supseteq I$. Suppose its error bound is successful, so the admissible doubly changed path $$Q=\\bigl(P_{r,b}-\\mathcal D(z)\\mathbf1_J,\n              P_{r,h}+\\mathcal D(z)z\\mathbf1_J\\bigr)$$ satisfies $$f_n(Q)+\\frac14\\int_J\\mathcal D(z)z^2\\,du\n             \\le f_n(P_r)+\\mathrm{err}.$$ Let $\\widehat r$ be a feasible parent trial, equal to $r$ outside the common working region, and suppose at every moved point $$\\widehat r=r+\\omega(z-r),\\qquad\n 0\\le\\omega<\\frac{\\mathcal D(z)}{e(r)+\\mathcal D(z)}.$$ If its changed path $P_{\\widehat r}$ is admissible, then $$\\int_I e(r)\\omega(z-r)^2\\,du\\le4\\,\\mathrm{err}.$$ This conclusion does not require an admissible bare parent endpoint or a parent error bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }

        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#reg:child-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"reg:child-data\">49</a>, let $y\\gtrsim x$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fl-chord",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $(b,h)$ and $(b+a,h-c)$ are bounded admissible paths, $a\\ge0$, and $c=0$ wherever $a=0$. If $c^2/a$ is integrable, with the quotient set to zero where $a=c=0$, then $$f_n(b+a,h-c)\\le f_n(b,h)+\\frac14\\int_0^1\\frac{c(u)^2}{a(u)}\\,du.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-217.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }
