    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fl-one-dimensional",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be centered with a log-concave density and $\\sigma^2=\\mathbb E X^2>0$. There are universal positive constants $c,C$ such that $$\\begin{split}\n &\\mathbb P\\{|X|\\ge t\\sigma\\}\\le2e^{-ct}\\quad(t\\ge0),\\\\\n &\\log\\mathbb E e^{tX}\\le C t^2\\sigma^2\n                       \\quad(0\\le t\\sigma\\le c),\\\\\n &\\mathbb P\\{X\\ge c\\sigma\\}\\ge c,\\qquad\n   \\log\\mathbb E e^{tX}\\ge ct\\sigma+\\log c\\quad(t\\ge0),\\\\\n &\\sup_x f_X(x)\\le C/\\sigma.\n\\end{split}$$ The lower log-moment inequality is understood also when the moment is infinite.",

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Gaussian SK Hamiltonian, partition function and log partition function** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a fixed inverse temperature $\beta>1$, define $$H_n(\sigma)=\frac{\beta}{\sqrt n}\sum_{1\le i<j\le n}g_{ij}\sigma_i\sigma_j, \qquad Z_n=2^{-n}\sum_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^n}e^{H_n(\sigma)}, \qquad F_n=\log Z_n.$$
- **Completed Hamiltonian and completed free energy** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Here $H_n^c=H_n+D$, where $D$ is an independent common Gaussian of variance $T/2$, and $F_n^c=F_n+D$.
- **Normalized spin and overlap** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Put $y(\sigma)=\sigma/\sqrt n$ and $R(\sigma,\tau)=y(\sigma)\cdot y(\tau)$.
- **Matrix and field feature maps** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The two feature maps $$\phi_b(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma\sigma^{\mathsf T}}{\sqrt{2n}}, \qquad \phi_h(\sigma)=\sigma$$ take values in Euclidean spaces of dimensions $n^2$ and $n$, respectively.
- **Self-corrected hierarchical pressure** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Set $$f_n(b,h)=\frac1n F_{-1}(0)-\frac{b_L}{4}-\frac{h_L}{2}.$$
- **Prefix conditional moments and pinned observable** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Define $$\begin{gathered} X_u=\mathbf E[y\mid\mathcal F_u],\qquad C_u=\mathbf E[yy^{\mathsf T}\mid\mathcal F_u]-X_uX_u^{\mathsf T},\\ S_u=\mathbf E\|X_u\|^2,\qquad B_u=\mathbf E\bigl\\|\mathbf E[yy^{\mathsf T}\mid\mathcal F_u]\bigr\\|_{\rm HS}^2, \qquad D_u^2=B_u-S_u^2,\\ L_u=y\cdot X_u-\tfrac12\|X_u\|^2. \end{gathered}$$
- **Outer regularity** ([source](#stmt-comp-outer-regularity)): A minimizer has a regular outer region above $x$ if, for fixed positive constants $c,C,C_0$, one has $cu\le r(u)\le Cu$ whenever $u\ge C_0x$. Enlarging the cutoff by a fixed factor gives the equivalent comparison $\alpha(r)\asymp r$ in radius coordinates. The constants are independent of $n$. No bound on a pointwise path derivative is part of this definition.
- **Admissible cavity tests** ([source](#stmt-def-cavity-tests)): A test of degree at most $N$ is a finite linear combination of products of centered boundary overlaps and their conditional projections, with these specifications. Increase $N$, if necessary, so that it also bounds the number of leaves after the fresh-replica representation of the projections. All low splits are $*$, except for at most one initial edge at a cut $i$. The distinguished target $a$ is unique in its block among the observed test leaves. Its dependence is a power of $$Y_a=X_*\cdot y_a-S_*$$ times at most one incoming boundary edge from an outside leaf. Other factors do not involve $a$. The second target $b$, when an identity is tested, is fresh at $*$ and absent from the test. An old block may contain several high relatives. Its internal relationships occur only in deterministic scalar coefficients and the prescribed tree topology; no overlap factor is internal to a cut block. High relationships between old leaves are integrated with finite-label attachment measures. Their bounded scalar coefficients, and constants obtained by expectations of earlier tests, are fixed during a subsequent row interpolation. For a monomial, its natural scale is the product of $W$ for each star factor and $x$ for the possible initial factor, including factors placed in a separate expectation. For a linear combination, $p_0$ is the sum of these scales multiplied by the absolute bounds on their scalar coefficients. The degree and the number of leaves are fixed independently of $n$; all tests generated below have these bounds controlled by the initial degree and recursion depth.
- **Admissible comparison families** ([source](#stmt-reg-families)): The working mass interval is $I=[u_0,1]$, where either $u_0=0$ or $u_0=o(a)$. For a feasible quantile $r$ satisfying <a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a>, write $$P_r=\bigl(b_0-e(r)\mathbf 1_I, h_0+e(r)r\mathbf 1_I\bigr),\qquad \mathcal P_e(r)=\frac14\int_I e(r)r^2\,du.$$ An ordinary comparison family has an admissible bare endpoint $(b_0,h_0)$ and admissible changed paths $P_r$ for every feasible $r$ and $0\le\lambda\le d^2$. Its minimized value is $\mathcal V(\lambda)=\min_r\{f_n(P_r)+\mathcal P_e(r)\}$. A fixed strength family requires admissibility of every $P_r$ only at the indicated positive strength. Its bare coefficients need not themselves define an admissible covariance path. Adding a term independent of $r$ does not change the minimizers or the profile conclusions. The notation $\mathcal V(\lambda)$ always denotes the displayed value without such an added term; all error bounds and strength derivatives refer to this value. Thus a term that depends on $\lambda$ must be subtracted at each strength before those bounds are applied. We require $d^2\le T-\rho$ for some fixed $\rho\in(0,T)$. The child coefficient $c_0$ introduced below is always chosen with $c_0^2\le T-\rho$, so this hypothesis persists in descendants. The recursive comparisons below obey one of two covariance conditions. Either they act on all masses with a fixed positive lower bound on the bare matrix covariance, or they act only on $I$, with a fixed positive matrix covariance on $I$ and a fixed positive matrix gap at $u_0$. A high-only family may be followed by all-mass families when the already changed endpoint has a fixed positive matrix lower bound. Once that change is made, all later descendants act on all masses. High-only descendants have lower quantile endpoint zero. Only the initial family may require a positive wall $r_{\min}$ as in <a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a>. At the initial stage the selected covariance alternative must have a fixed margin after the initial comparison as well. For example, an all-mass bare matrix lower bound $T/2$ and $d^2\le T/4$ give such a margin. In the high-only case the analogous requirement is a fixed residual high covariance and high-to-low gap. Alternatively, a fixed global lower bound after the initial high-only comparison allows the immediate change to all-mass descendants.
- **Scalar effective covariance path** ([source](#sec:profile)): Put $$K_u=h_u+b_uS_u.$$
- **Centered completed log-Laplace transform** ([source](#sec:fluctuations)): Consequently $$p(0)=\frac1n\mathbb EF_n^c-\frac T4,\qquad p(T)=\frac1{ns}\log\mathbb E e^{sF_n^c}-\frac T4, \qquad K_n(s):=\log\mathbb E e^{s(F_n^c-\mathbb EF_n^c)} =ns\,[p(T)-p(0)].$$

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-log-partition-standard-deviation | Standard deviation of the log partition function | = | $n^{1/6+o(1)}$ | Temperature is fixed before the size limit. |
| q-log-partition-variance | Variance of the log partition function | = | $n^{1/3+o(1)}$ | Arbitrarily small fixed power slack; no constant prefactor is supplied. |
| q-pinned-second-moment-constant | Pinned second-moment constant | = | $1$ | Deterministic weights in the pinned moment estimate. |
| q-pinned-real-moment-constant | Pinned moment constant | ≤ | $1+p^2/3$ | The moment order is fixed. |
| q-single-label-attachment-variation | Total variation of a single-label attachment | = | $2r-a$ | The enclosing block mass and observed leaf count are fixed for the allocation. |
| q-cut-layer-variation-coefficient | Permissible cut-layer variation coefficient | = | $12q$ | Coefficients must obey the stated deeper-placement independence condition. |
| q-comparison-budget-ceiling | Comparison-error budget | ≤ | $n^{-1/2}d^2$ | The lower budget condition and fixed auxiliary exponents remain required. |
| q-lower-direct-test-scale | Lower direct-test scale | = | $\frac{\sqrt n E}{d}$ | Comparison parameters obey the full budget range. |
| q-smallest-mass-constraint-scale | Smallest-mass constraint scale | = | $\frac1{nE}$ | The budget is positive in the stipulated comparison range. |
| q-total-floor-mass | Total floor mass | ≤ | $n^{-\delta}u_*$ | This controls mass and does not by itself control radius length. |
| q-good-window-overlap-deviation | Centered overlap norm in the good split window | ≤ | $C_p n^{-c_2}v$ | The polynomial saving is independent of the fixed moment order. |
| q-swept-comparison-error | Minimized comparison excess | ≤ | $n^{20\delta}E$ | The error assertion concerns ordinary sweeps; fixed-strength roots receive only the profile assertion. |
| q-child-budget-growth | Child comparison-error budget | ≥ | $n^{\delta/4}E$ | The child budget is defined by the minimum of its ceiling and separation-cost budgets. |
| q-laplace-evaluation-scale | Positive Laplace evaluation scale | = | $n^{-1/6+5m}$ | Temperature and the auxiliary exponent are fixed before the size limit. |
| q-positive-laplace-lower-window | Centered completed log-Laplace transform at the stipulated scale | ≥ | $c_{T,m}n^{20m}$ | The positive transform alone is not a variance or small-ball conclusion. |
| q-positive-laplace-upper-window | Centered completed log-Laplace transform at the stipulated scale | ≤ | $C_{T,m}n^{100m}$ | The exponent and temperature remain fixed. |
| q-exponential-augmentation-variance | Variance of the exponentially augmented completed free energy | = | $\operatorname{Var}F_n^c+1$ | The variance identity does not require a transform parameter. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-source-pressure-pinned-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-found-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-found-moments): Differentiate normalized continuations, telescope Gaussian covariance derivatives, then pin a terminal spin and apply the inverse-Hessian variance argument with gauge-invariant centering. -> Source covariance representation, pressure and susceptibility identities, and unconditioned single-path moments.
- **step-real-mass-signed-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-found-taylor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-found-replica): Apply labeled product rules to positive real powers, identify attachment measures, bound split densities and iterate fixed covariance insertions. -> Covariance differentiation, exact row sums, bounded attachment variation, split-density control, Taylor bounds and source-derivative bounds.
- **step-cut-coupling-boundary-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-found-boundary-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-found-coupling): Collapse placement-independent tails, pair laws preserving the old marginal, center using their common conditional marginals and telescope boundary polynomials. -> Cut-layer variation, centered resampling bounds and closure of boundary polynomial estimates.
- **step-bounded-path-extension** [claim](#stmt-prop-found-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-found-limits): Approximate monotone clocks by steps retaining terminal values, cut sides and prescribed joint jump fillings, then use continuity and dominated convergence. -> Fixed-size path limits for planted expectations, derivatives and integrated formulas.
- **step-envelope-and-first-variation** [claim](#stmt-comp-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-geometry): Use compactness of inverse quantiles for attainment and a constrained first variation for the potential governing cap, contact and floor regions. -> Minimizer existence, the optimized envelope inequality and geometric mismatch and width bounds.
- **step-projection-and-raw-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-comp-raw-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-projection-2): Derive a field-clock projection estimate, decompose synchronized martingale layers and absorb the remaining raw-moment bootstrap term. -> Raw moments throughout the path and postprojection resampling bounds.
- **step-deterministic-good-split** [claim](#stmt-comp-good-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-good-split): Select a deterministic window using small mean-overlap oscillation, then apply buffer averages, projection and unconditional moment estimates. -> A split window with a polynomial saving independent of each later fixed moment order.
- **step-whole-unit-trace-and-level-averaging** [claim](#stmt-comp-bracket) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-bracket): Keep complete caps as sampling units, control their averaged traces and compare each evaluation point with ordered side averages. -> Integrated dispersion bounds without a pointwise cap-trace assumption.
- **step-local-errors-in-both-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-comp-direct-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-bracket-4): Apply level averaging on outer dyads and inner groups, account for broad caps and walls, and estimate radius averages directly rather than discarding low-mass radius intervals. -> Local second moments, mass and radius dispersion control, good-cut refinement and conditional comparison error.
- **step-cavity-transfer-and-boundary-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cavity-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cavity-boundary): Transfer fixed spin tests across bounded row changes and sum observed-block attachment layers before applying centered boundary resampling estimates. -> Row-transfer control, observed-block truncation cancellation and a small boundary covariance correction.
- **step-finite-degree-row-expansion** [claim](#stmt-lem-cavity-row-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cavity-row-identity): Expand low revelation and high row interpolation separately, retain parity cancellations and process observed and unobserved intermediate blocks with their distinct attachment rules. -> An admissible row identity with a uniform target remainder and projected replacements.
- **step-longitudinal-recursion-and-calibration** [claim](#stmt-lem-cavity-calibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cavity-calibration): Subtract a fresh-root identity to eliminate the same-degree nuisance, iterate marked star factors to fixed depth and calibrate the remaining susceptibility defect with the constant test. -> Calibrated longitudinal insertion bounds.
- **step-target-and-coordinate-closure** [claim](#stmt-prop-cavity-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cavity-calibration-2): Return the calibrated mean identity to the target cut, control cutoff changes by integrated errors and average the uniform target modulus in both coordinates. -> Uniform one-site closure, omitted-group bounds and normalized mass and radius closure.
- **step-scalar-compactness-and-linearization** [claim](#stmt-prof-scalar-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-scalar-compactness): Use scalar diffusion derivative identities and strict curvature, select weak limits and calibrate the scalar defect at a positive outer clock. -> A common limiting clock distribution, a scalar defect equation and uniform low-clock linearization.
- **step-virtual-gap-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-prof-direct-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-virtual-potential-2): Pass the first-variation potential to virtual radius intervals and compare its endpoint sign restrictions with the scalar defect equation to exclude support gaps. -> A positive linear small-scale quantile profile.
- **step-macroscopic-parisi-identification** [claim](#stmt-prof-macro-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-macro-profile): Pass an optimized interpolation to a scalar minimum, prove uniqueness by stochastic-control strict convexity and complete the square in the limiting joint objective. -> The unique scalar minimizer, its positive initial density and macroscopic quantile rigidity.
- **step-child-data-and-feasible-agreement** [claim](#stmt-reg-successful-child) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reg-successful-child): Choose larger child budgets, construct feasible parent barriers and compare movement gains with a successful child error. -> Valid child data and profile transfer whenever the child error succeeds.
- **step-finite-regularity-propagation** [claim](#stmt-reg-finite-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reg-finite-propagation): Follow a hypothetical profile failure through failed child errors, increasing the budget until its allowed range is exhausted at fixed depth. -> Global outer regularity, ordinary-family sweep errors and uniform wall profiles.
- **step-positive-laplace-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-fl-laplace-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fl-wall-cost): Combine admissible chord comparisons with a lower comparison, an initial upper comparison and successive high comparisons using a low-field wall. -> Wall admissibility, low-group mean suppression, sweep-cost bounds and the positive Laplace window.
- **step-augmentation-and-fluctuation-transfer** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fl-one-dimensional-2): Obtain log-concavity by exponential augmentation and gauge symmetry, apply log-concave moment and density estimates, then remove the independent augmentation noises. -> The standard-deviation, variance and typical centered-magnitude exponents.

Its Hessian is the sum of the remaining conditional martingale covariance increments, weighted by the mass of each transition, with mass $1$ for the final spin draw. In particular, the Hessian $H_u$ in a normalized spin source satisfies $$uC_u\\preceq H_u\\preceq C_u.$$ If $y_1,y_2$ are independent continuations from the prefix at $u$, then $$\\mathbf E(y_1\\cdot y_2)=S_u,\\qquad\n \\mathbf E(y_1\\cdot y_2)^2=B_u,$$ and hence $D_u$ is the standard deviation of this overlap.           "exact_quote": "Let $K_\\theta(u;\\sigma,\\tau)$ be an affine family of cumulative Gaussian covariance kernels of a finite hierarchy. Assume that the independent increment covariances are positive semidefinite throughout the parameter interval. Let $V=\\partial_\\theta K_\\theta$ and let $P$ be a fixed bounded spin test on a fixed observed split tree. Then $$\\frac{d}{d\\theta}\\nu_\\theta[P]\n      =\\frac12\\sum_{c,d}\\nu_\\theta[P V_{cd}].\n \\tag{2}\\label{eq:2}$$ The signed sum includes coincidences with old labels and between $c,d$. For distinct labels, $V_{cd}$ is evaluated at their common revelation; on the diagonal it is evaluated at the terminal covariance. The exact weights are the real-power weights described in the proof. A constant diagonal contribution, independent of the summation label, sums to zero. Conditional means in $P$ can be represented by independent additional replicas at their specified prefixes before this formula is applied.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-attachment",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an enclosing block of mass $a\\in[0,1]$ containing $r\\geq1$ observed leaves. After grouping placements which induce the same extended tree, one free summation label has the following signed attachment measure:*\n\n1.  *weight $+1$ for coincidence with each observed leaf;*\n\n2.  *measure $-dm$ on every occupied open edge, parameterized by its increasing mass coordinate;*\n\n3.  *weight $(1-d_v)u_v$ at an observed fork of mass $u_v$ with $d_v$ occupied children, including an initial fork if present.*\n\n*The total signed mass is $a$ and the total variation is $2r-a\\leq2r$. For an empty block the first label has weight $a$. Consequently, adjoining $k$ labels to a tree with $r\\geq1$ old labels has total variation at most $$2^k r(r+1)\\cdots(r+k-1).$$ Adjoining $k\\geq1$ labels in a previously empty block has variation at most $a\\,2^{k-1}(k-1)!$. These bounds are independent of the mass partition. A free row whose summand is independent of the detailed attachment inside a block of mass $u$ collapses exactly to $u$ times that summand.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-split-density",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite observed tree with at most $q$ leaves and allocate at most $k$ new labels. Apart from coincidences and atoms at specified old forks or cuts, the split mass of any specified pair involving a new label has a Lebesgue density with total variation bounded by a constant $C_{q,k}$. The same statement holds when some old fork masses are themselves integrated against a measure of bounded density, with the resulting bound multiplied by that density bound. These assertions concern the signed attachment measure and its variation, not the size of a spin observable at a pointwise split level.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"

          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:found-replica\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:found-replica\">6</a>, suppose $P$ uses at most $q$ labels and $|V(u;\\sigma,\\tau)|\\leq M$. For each fixed integer $j\\geq1$ there is $C_{q,j}<\\infty$, independent of the number of covariance steps, such that $$\\left|\\frac{d^j}{d\\theta^j}\\nu_\\theta[P]\\right|\n       \\leq C_{q,j}M^j\\|P\\|_\\infty.$$ More precisely the derivative is an integral over a signed measure of extended trees with uniformly bounded total variation, of expectations of $P$ times $j$ covariance insertions, with the factor $2^{-j}$. Hölder’s inequality can therefore be applied to these integrands under their genuine probability laws. If $P\\geq0$, then $$\\left|\\frac{d}{d\\theta}\\nu_\\theta[P]\\right|\n        \\leq C_{q,1}M\\nu_\\theta[P],$$ and hence $$e^{-C_{q,1}M|\\theta-\\theta'|}\\nu_{\\theta'}[P]\n \\leq\\nu_\\theta[P]\n \\leq e^{C_{q,1}M|\\theta-\\theta'|}\\nu_{\\theta'}[P].$$ The assertions require that the test, including any scalar centers in it, be fixed during the interpolation.           "exact_quote": "Fix an old tree with at most $q$ leaves in a block of mass $U$, and a free row label $r$ in that block. Let $U\\leq v\\leq w\\leq\\min(2v,1)$. The difference of its signed row tests truncated at $w$ and at $v$ can, after exact grouping of the deeper placements, be written as a signed combination of pairs of planted laws with total coefficient variation at most $C_qv$. In each pair the old-label tree is the same, and only the coupling of $r$ to one occupied descendant subtree after the revelation at $v$ differs. One may take $C_q=12q$.*\n\n*The statement remains true when the row is multiplied by a bounded coefficient depending on the fixed old topology, multiplied into the bound by its supremum. For the exact collapse of deeper placements this coefficient, and all scalar centers in the row test, must be independent of $r$’s detailed attachment beyond the truncation cut. Allocation-order symmetry carries the full topology coefficient with it; it does not establish the independence required for a free-row collapse. At each recursion generation, freeze the previously generated topology, its coefficients, and earlier scalar centers before introducing fresh slots. A current coefficient depending on the row’s deeper placement must remain explicit until eliminated or estimated. Only after this row calculation may the enlarged topology be frozen for the next generation.",

Let $\\mathcal G$ be the sigma-field generated by their complete common prefix through the prescribed revelation at $v$, the realizations of all old branches outside the affected descendant subtree after $v$, and the deterministic old topology. No information from the continuation of $r$ or from the affected old subtree after $v$ is included in $\\mathcal G$. Let $F$ be a function of $r$ and this exterior information, and let $P$ be a function of the old subtree and the exterior information. The two laws have the same conditional marginal law of $F$ given $\\mathcal G$ and the same conditional marginal law of $P$ given $\\mathcal G$. Write their common conditional means as $\\bar F,\\bar P$. Their difference therefore obeys $$(\\mathbf E_1-\\mathbf E_0)[FP]\n   =(\\mathbf E_1-\\mathbf E_0)[(F-\\bar F)(P-\\bar P)].$$ If $p^{-1}+p'^{-1}=1$, its absolute value is at most $$\\sum_{i=0}^1\\|F-\\bar F\\|_{L^p(\\mathbf P_i)}\n                  \\|P-\\bar P\\|_{L^{p'}(\\mathbf P_i)}.$$ Each centered norm is at most the corresponding norm of the difference from an independent resampling with the same conditional marginal given $\\mathcal G$. Consequently uniform resampling bounds $A_v,B_v$ for these two factors give a bound $2C_qvA_vB_v$ for the full cut layer.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-found-boundary-tests",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a cut $U$ and its old descendant blocks. Let $P$ be a polynomial of fixed degree in overlaps between different blocks, centered by fixed scalars, and in products of such overlaps with fresh auxiliary leaves split at specified cuts. Assume that each overlap involving an affected old subtree has its other endpoint outside that subtree. Bounded coefficients may depend on the entire fixed old topology. All scalar centers may be expectations on an earlier tree, provided they are held fixed during the current row interpolation.*\n\n*On resampling the affected subtree while retaining its prefix, only the overlap factors crossing its boundary vary. If each such factor has a resampling-difference bound in the required finite $L^p$ norm, and the other factors have their required finite moment bounds, then $P$ has the bound obtained by the product rule, with a constant depending only on its degree, number of monomials, and coefficient bound. In particular this property is preserved by multiplying the current target test by centered overlaps to fresh auxiliary leaves, and by introducing a fresh target block joined to an earlier target by one overlap. Earlier target blocks may have several incident boundary overlaps.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }
      ],

      "inputs": [
        "Signed attachment row sums",

      "method": "Collapse placement-independent tails, pair laws preserving the old marginal, center using their common conditional marginals and telescope boundary polynomials.",
      "output": "Cut-layer variation, centered resampling bounds and closure of boundary polynomial estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-found-coupling"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-found-limits",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-found-limits",
          "exact_quote": "The preceding construction and estimates extend to bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths $b,h$ by step approximation. The limit is independent of the approximations, provided the terminal clocks converge and the before/after clocks at every specified cut converge to the specified values. Planted expectations of bounded spin tests on a fixed finite split tree with cuts before or after jumps, fixed-order source derivatives, the signed calculus, and the mass-integrated pressure and moment formulas have these limits. The susceptibility inequality holds on the continuous parts, with nonnegative total increments of $S$ at jumps. For intermediate cuts and the separate clock contributions inside a filled jump, use approximations retaining the prescribed joint filling; no independence from the choice of that filling is asserted. All constants in the finite-tree variation and Taylor bounds are unchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-bounded-path-extension",
      "inputs": [
        "Partition-independent source and signed-calculus bounds",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-envelope",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $n$, the class <a href=\"#eq:5\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:5\">[eq:5]</a> is nonempty and compact in the uniform topology of inverse quantiles. The minimum in <a href=\"#eq:6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:6\">[eq:6]</a> is attained. For an admissible sweep, $\\mathcal M_\\lambda$ is locally Lipschitz and nondecreasing in $\\lambda$, equals the endpoint pressure at $\\lambda=0$, and is an upper bound for that pressure. At almost every positive strength its upper derivative per unit $\\log\\lambda$ is bounded, at any minimizer at that strength, by $$\\frac14\\int_{u_0}^1 e(r(u))\n           \\bigl[D_u^2+(S_u-r(u))^2\\bigr]\\,du.\n \\tag{7}\\label{eq:7}$$ All moments in this formula are for the changed clocks in <a href=\"#eq:6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:6\">[eq:6]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-geometry",
          "exact_quote": "At a minimizer there is a continuous absolutely continuous potential $H$ such that $H'=JG$ for a strictly positive factor $J$, $\\alpha'=M(\\alpha)$ on $\\{H<0\\}$, and $\\alpha'=k$ on $\\{H>0\\}$. On a band where $\\alpha+u_*$ varies by a bounded factor, $J$ has bounded distortion after normalization at one endpoint. On the contact set $\\{H=0\\}$, almost everywhere, $$G=0,\\qquad S'\\lesssim n^{o(1)},\\qquad S\\le r.\n \\tag{9}\\label{eq:9}$$ On an open cap component $\\{H<0\\}$ of radius length $\\ell$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\Delta S\\lesssim n^{o(1)}\n        (\\ell+r_{\\min}\\mathbf1_{\\mathrm{initial}}),\n \\qquad S\\le r+\\ell,\\\\\n |S-r|^2\\lesssim n^{o(1)}\n   \\left[D^2+\\ell^2+r_{\\min}^2\\mathbf1_{\\mathrm{initial}}\\right].\n \\end{gathered}\n \\tag{10}\\label{eq:10}$$ Here $\\Delta S$ is the increase through the interior of the component. On contact, $(S-r)^2\\lesssim D^2$. Every cap has $\\ell\\lesssim n^{-\\delta}L\\log n$; if it is contained in a comparable mass band $u\\asymp v\\gg u_*$, then $v\\ell\\lesssim n^{-\\delta}E/d^3$. A noninitial cap on which $p=0$ throughout satisfies $|S-r|\\le\\ell$. The total floor mass is at most $n^{-\\delta}u_*$.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-comp-raw-moments",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-raw-moments",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $p\\ge2$, throughout the path, including masses below the working interval, $$\\|X_u\\|_p\\lesssim_p\\sqrt{u+x},\\qquad\n \\|R_{\\mathrm{split\\ at}\\ u}\\|_p\\lesssim_p u+x.\n \\tag{12}\\label{eq:12}$$ The bounds also hold at intermediate clock revelations, with their specified mass. The norm of a random vector is its Euclidean norm inside the $L^p$ norm.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-projection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a vector determined by outside branches and by data at or before their fork with a branch $b$. Conditional on this data, the future of $b$ has its usual planted law. Suppose a field-clock interval after the fork has length $D_h$, with mass at least $v_->0$ throughout. For even fixed $p\\ge2$, the part of $V\\cdot y_b$ left after projection at the end of the interval satisfies $$\\|V\\cdot(y_b-X_{b,\\mathrm{end}})\\|_p\n \\le C_p(\\log n)^2\\frac{\\|V\\|_p}{v_-\\sqrt{nD_h}}.\n \\tag{13}\\label{eq:13}$$ Here the masses and clock lengths used in the estimate are bounded below by inverse powers of $n$. Differences of two later projections satisfy the same estimate up to a constant.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-resampling",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose two leaves share only through a mass at most a fixed multiple of $x_1$, where $x_1\\ge x$. If a projection or resampling is made after a regular radius $v\\ge Cx_1$, with comparable intervals fitting after the fork and before that projection, the change in their inner product caused by changing one endpoint has norm $$\\frac{C_p(\\log n)^C}{\\sqrt{ne(v)v^2}}\n \\tag{16}\\label{eq:16}$$ in every fixed $L^p$. For an already projected anchor of norm $O_p(\\sqrt{x_1})$, the bound gains a factor $\\sqrt{x_1/v}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"

          "anchor": "stmt-comp-good-split",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a regular band of mass and radius of order $v\\ge Cx$ contains a mass interval of length comparable to $v$ on which $S\\asymp v$. There are constants $c_1,c_2>0$, independent of each fixed moment order, and a deterministic window of mass length $n^{-c_1}v$ with the following properties. Its fixed-factor enlargement has $S$ oscillation at most $n^{-c_1/2}v$, and for every deterministic split in a fixed central fraction of the window, $$\\|R_*-S_*\\|_p\\le C_p n^{-c_2}v\n \\tag{17}\\label{eq:17}$$ for all fixed $p\\ge2$.           "anchor": "stmt-comp-unit-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a group at comparable masses $u\\asymp v$ of total mass at most $Cv$, and suppose the radii and the raw overlap moments in that group are bounded at scale $y_0$. Away from the floor, take as units complete cap components with comparable endpoint masses, and mass elements of the contact set. Let $e_g$ be a lower bound on $e$ in the group. If a cap has no initial-wall loss, then within each unit $$\\operatorname{avg}_{\\mathrm{unit}}\n       \\mathbb E\\operatorname{tr}H_u^2\n \\lesssim\\frac{n^{o(1)}}{ne_g}.\n \\tag{20}\\label{eq:20}$$ On contact this holds pointwise almost everywhere. In particular the unit averages of $\\mathbb E\\operatorname{tr}C_u^2$ and $\\mathbb EW_u$ are at most $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv^2)$ and $n^{o(1)}/(ne_gv)$, respectively.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-bracket",
          "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#comp:unit-trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:unit-trace\">26</a>, let $\\ell_0$ be a common polynomial-scale upper bound for cap widths, including a possible wall term when it is covered by the trace estimate. Suppose on the group, for every fixed $p$, the raw bounds are $\\|X\\|_p\\le C_p\\sqrt{y_0}$ and $\\|R\\|_p\\le C_py_0$. Then, up to a factor $n^{O(\\epsilon')}$ for any fixed $\\epsilon'>0$, and an edge error $O(k_0n^{-10})$, $$\\int_{\\mathrm{group}}D_u^2\\,du\n \\lesssim v\\left[\n       (y_0/k_0)^2+y_0\\ell_0+\\ell_0^2\n              +\\frac{k_0+1}{ne_gv^2}\\right],\n \\qquad 1\\le k_0\\le n^2.\n \\tag{21}\\label{eq:21}$$ In particular the first and last variable terms may be replaced by $C[(y_0/(ne_gv^2))^{2/3}+1/(ne_gv^2)]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-comp-direct-moments",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-whole-unit-trace-and-level-averaging",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-direct-moments",
          "exact_quote": "On every regular outer dyad of mass $u\\asymp v$ beyond a sufficiently large fixed multiple of $x$, for every sufficiently small fixed $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\left(\\operatorname{avg}_{u\\asymp v}D_u^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\lesssim_\\epsilon n^\\epsilon\\left[\n (ne(v)v)^{-1/3}+(ne(v)v^2)^{-1/2}\n +(n^{-\\delta}E/d^3)^{1/2}+n^{\\delta/2}u_*\\right].\n \\tag{18}\\label{eq:18}$$ Fixed enlargements of a dyad are permitted in this assertion.*\n\n\n*For a small direct test impose additionally $e(x)\\gtrsim x^2$, and set $W=\\max(x,\\sqrt{e(x)})$. Then $W\\lesssim d$ and $e(v)\\gtrsim W^2$ for $v=O(W)$. On regular dyads of scale at least a fixed multiple of $W$, including comparable dyads at $W$ that lie in the regular region, the right side of <a href=\"#eq:18\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:18\">[eq:18]</a> is polynomially smaller than $x^2/W$. Between $Cx$ and $O(W)$ it is polynomially smaller than $x^2/(W^2v)^{1/3}$. Moreover $$\\int_0^{CW}D_u\\,du=o(x^2),\\qquad D=o(x).\n \\tag{19}\\label{eq:19}$$ The integral is truncated at mass $1$, and $C$ may be any fixed constant. The second assertion means normalized mean-square convergence on every compact band $r\\le Cx$, $cx\\le u\\le Cx$, both in mass and in radius: $$x^{-3}\\int_{\\{r\\le Cx,\\,cx\\le u\\le Cx\\}}D^2\\,du\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n x^{-3}\\int_{\\{r\\le Cx,\\,cx\\le\\alpha(r)\\le Cx\\}}D^2\\,dr\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ If $W=o(1)$, there is a split $*$ in a regular band of scale $W$ with $S_*\\asymp W$, satisfying <a href=\"#eq:17\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:17\">[eq:17]</a>, and with $D_*$ polynomially smaller than $x^2/W$. It can be chosen above any fixed multiple of $x$.",

        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-conditional-error",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda\\ge n^{-10}$ and $A=A_\\lambda=n^\\delta\\sqrt n E/\\sqrt\\lambda$. If $A=o(1)$, assume that a minimizer has a regular outer region above $A$. If $A$ stays bounded below by a positive constant, the same conclusion holds without a regularity hypothesis.*\n\n*Consequently, if these hypotheses hold along an admissible sweep from zero to any strength at most $d^2$, then $0\\le\\mathcal M_\\lambda-f_n(b_0,h_0)\\le n^{20\\delta}E$.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-row-change",
          "exact_quote": "Along a segment of admissible changes of one Gaussian row with uniformly bounded covariance kernels, expectations of nonnegative spin-only tests on at most $N$ leaves change by at most a factor $C_N$, independently of the step partition. The raw moment bounds and the actual-path centered-overlap bounds above therefore hold throughout such a segment with this factor.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-truncation",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an old tree with at most $N$ leaves in a block of mass $U$. Deterministic scalar coefficients may depend on all its old split values and earlier test expectations, but not on a new leaf $c$’s deeper placement. Replace $c$, after an occupied prefix of mass $v$, by an independent continuation from that prefix. The signed sum of deeper placements having this same truncated value is $v$ times that value. The telescoping difference between truncations at adjacent comparable scales $v,2v$ has total variation at most $C_Nv$. The final layer may include coincidences.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-boundary",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F_c$ be a product of two factors of the form $h_j+b_jR_{cd}$, where $d$ is exterior to the block of $c$ and their low split satisfies $j=j(c,d)\\in\\{i,*\\}$; one exterior endpoint may be conditionally projected. Let $P$ be admissible. If no overlap edge of $P$ is internal to the old block in which $c$ is allocated, the placement sum after subtracting the fresh-at-cut baseline is $o(Wx^2p_0)$, uniformly at fixed degree.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-row-identity",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-family",
          "exact_quote": "Centering an admissible test, multiplying by a projected star factor at its target, or passing to a fresh target and multiplying by one centered star edge to a summed high relative in the former target block preserves Definition <a href=\"#def:cavity-tests\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:cavity-tests\">30</a> at a larger finite degree. New coefficients and attachment measures are bounded in terms of the resulting degree.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-row-identity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $a,b$ split at $*$, with $b$ fresh, and let $P$ be admissible of degree $N$. Let $A$ and $B^{\\prime}$ be the blocks at the current cut $U$ containing $a$ and $b$, respectively. Then $$\\label{eq:24}\\tag{24}\n \\begin{split}\n \\nu[R_{ab}P]={}&\\Gamma(K_*)\\nu[P]\n  +b_*\\sum_{c\\in A,d\\in B'}\\gamma_{ac}\\gamma_{bd}\n                                             \\nu[\\Delta_{cd}P]\\\\\n &+\\sum_{c\\in A,d\\in B'}\n   \\nu[P(G_{ac}\\gamma_{bd}+\\gamma_{ac}G_{bd})\n                                     (K_*+b_*\\Delta_{cd})]\n   +\\mathcal R_N(P).\n \\end{split}$$ At fixed degree, uniformly over initial targets $i$ in the band of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cavity-closure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cavity-closure\">29</a>, the remainder satisfies $$|\\mathcal R_N(P)|\\le\n C_NWx^2p_0\\{\\eta_{N,n}+(D_i/x)^{\\vartheta_N}\\},\\qquad\n       \\eta_{N,n}\\to0,\\quad\\vartheta_N>0,$$ where the $D_i$ term is absent without an initial factor. Consequently $\\mathcal R_N(P)=o(Wx^2p_0)$ for star-only tests and along every moving initial target with $D_i=o(x)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-replacements",
          "exact_quote": "After averaging the fresh partner block, $$\\sum_{c\\in A}\\gamma_{ac}\\nu[Y_cP]\n              =\\chi\\nu[Y_aP]+o(Wx^2p_0).$$ In the centered part multiplying $G_{ac}$, $Y_c$ can likewise be replaced by $Y_a$, with error $o(Wx^2p_0)$. In the part multiplying $G_{bd}$, interchanging $b,d$ and summing that block gives exactly $\\sum_c\\gamma_{ac}\\mathcal L(P\\Delta_{bc},b)$. The uniform target modulus of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:cavity-row-identity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:cavity-row-identity\">35</a> applies.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-recursion",
          "exact_quote": "Put $J_0=T\\chi^2-1$. Every admissible fixed-degree test satisfies, uniformly over initial targets $i$ in the band of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cavity-closure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cavity-closure\">29</a>, $$|\\mathcal L(P,a)|\\le\n Cx^2p_0\\{\\eta_n+(D_i/x)^\\vartheta+\\eta_n|J_0|/W^2\\},\n                       \\qquad \\eta_n\\to0,\\quad\\vartheta>0.$$ The $D_i$ term is absent for star-only tests. Consequently, for such tests and along moving initial targets with $D_i=o(x)$, $$\\label{eq:26}\\tag{26}\n       \\mathcal L(P,a)=o\\bigl(x^2p_0(1+|J_0|/W^2)\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cavity-calibration",
          "exact_quote": "One has $J_0=O(W^2)$. Therefore $$\\label{eq:27}\\tag{27}\n                         \\mathcal L(P,a)=o(x^2p_0)$$ for admissible tests along moving targets with $D_i=o(x)$, and uniformly $$|\\mathcal L(P,a)|\\le Cx^2p_0\\{\\eta_n+(D_i/x)^\\vartheta\\}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        }

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-cavity-closure",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-longitudinal-recursion-and-calibration",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cavity-closure",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<c<C<\\infty$. At a target cut $i$ satisfying $r_i\\le Cx$ and $cx\\le u_i\\le Cx$, along every sequence with $D_i=o(x)$, $$\\label{eq:22}\\tag{22}\n                         S_i=\\Gamma(K_i)+o(x^3).$$ The bound is $O(x^3)$, uniformly over these targets, if $D_i=O(x)$. More precisely, for a fixed direct-test sequence there are $\\eta_n\\to0$, $C_0<\\infty$, and $\\vartheta>0$, independent of the target in this band, such that $$\\frac{|S_i-\\Gamma(K_i)|}{x^3}\n       \\le C_0\\{\\eta_n+(D_i/x)^\\vartheta\\}.$$ When $W=O(x)$, the $O(x^3)$ assertion also holds at every omitted lower cut, without a lower bound on its mass and without <a href=\"#eq:4\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:4\">[eq:4]</a> at that cut. On the bands in <a href=\"#eq:19\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:19\">[eq:19]</a>, the error in <a href=\"#eq:22\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:22\">[eq:22]</a>, divided by $x^3$, tends to zero in normalized $L^1$, in both mass and radius coordinates.",
          "source_id": "PM-217.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prof-coordinate-closure",
          "exact_quote": "On each such band, $$\\frac1{x^3}\\int_{I_n(c,C)}D(r)^2\\,dr\\longrightarrow0.$$ The analogous statement holds with physical mass in place of radius. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-fixed-temperature-fluctuation-exponent**: The standard deviation and typical centered absolute fluctuations have the stated power exponent for the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at fixed finite low temperature.
- **invariant-unconditioned-single-path-moments**: The pinned moment estimate controls the full unconditioned planted path and its terminal spin at zero deterministic external field, using deterministic weights.
- **invariant-signed-row-collapse**: A free row collapses to its enclosing mass only when its remaining summand is independent of the detailed attachment within that block. Signed grouping precedes absolute bounds.
- **invariant-projection-preserves-planted-future**: Projection estimates require an exterior anchor whose conditioning leaves the selected branch with its entire normalized planted future law.
- **invariant-conditional-error-before-propagation**: The local comparison error requires outer regularity when its cutoff tends to zero. A bound for the full sweep additionally requires admissibility throughout that sweep.
- **invariant-exponential-augmentation**: Adding the independent exponential variable to the completed free energy produces a log-concave density and preserves the exact transform and variance identities stated for the augmentation.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-limiting-law-or-prefactor**: Convert the exponent theorem into a numerical limiting distribution or a variance prefactor. -> Power exponents do not identify a limiting law or a nonzero constant prefactor.
- **anti-use-growing-orders-or-depth**: Increase the number of replicas, moment orders, or recursion depth with the system size. -> Constants and thresholds may depend on these fixed choices, so the stated estimates do not control growing orders.
- **anti-use-prefix-conditioned-pinning**: Apply the pinned moment inequality after conditioning on an arbitrary realized prefix or after spin-dependent reweighting. -> The unconditioned marginal and its centering are replaced by a law outside the moment estimate.
- **anti-use-premature-absolute-attachment-sum**: Take absolute values of individual attachment coefficients and claim the same small-mass saving as the signed row sum. -> Cancellation can disappear before the enclosing-mass factor is obtained.
- **anti-use-selected-branch-anchor-leakage**: Build the exterior projection anchor from unrevealed variables of the selected continuation. -> Conditioning can change the selected branch’s future law and invalidate the innovation estimate.
- **anti-use-mass-only-gap-control**: Discard a floor interval in radius because its physical mass is small, or replace a cap-average trace bound by a pointwise trace bound. -> A negligible mass interval can retain appreciable radius length, and an average does not control every point.
- **anti-use-positive-transform-alone**: Infer variance and typical fluctuation magnitude directly from a positive Laplace window. -> Rare deviations can dominate the transform or variance without supplying the required typical-magnitude control.
- **anti-use-temperature-endpoint-uniformity**: Use the sequential temperature extension as a uniform estimate approaching the critical temperature or with growing recursion depth. -> The sequential extension retains fixed margins and a limiting temperature strictly above the transition.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The selected target is the asymptotic fluctuation exponent and typical-magnitude theorem at fixed temperature. Its quantification includes "More explicitly, for every $\eta>0$ there is $n_0(\beta,\eta)$ such that" at stmt-thm-main. The proof uses subsequence compactness, variational limits, and asymptotic error estimates; finite execution cannot decide this eventual statement. The auxiliary finite-tree attachment identities do not certify the asymptotic fluctuation conclusion. As stated at pm-h-43, "The exponent theorem supplies neither a limiting law nor a variance prefactor; those are separate conclusions of the companion." A finite constructive certificate for the selected target is ABSENT.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source derivative identities describe conditional expectations in Gaussian hierarchies. They do not provide a software interface or a procedure for computing those expectations. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fluctuation theorem describes asymptotic disorder fluctuations in the specified spin model. It supplies neither a simulation transition rule nor a finite-size calibration for a simulation. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The passage from step hierarchies to bounded monotone paths establishes analytic limits while preserving specified cut values. It supplies no certified discretization tolerance for an executable evaluation tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite propagation is a contradiction argument following hypothetical profile and error failures. Its fixed-depth proof does not provide an executable method for selecting minimizing quantiles or detecting those failures. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The signed attachment measure specifies mathematical weights on observed trees, including fork atoms and coincidences. It does not specify a representation supporting computational tree operations or storage guarantees. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The normalized feature maps realize the interaction and field covariances mathematically. Their Euclidean realization supplies no accelerator architecture, arithmetic specification, or hardware execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coupling lemma compares planted probability laws with precisely matched conditional marginals. It establishes no adversarial security property or cryptographic construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scalar minimizer result concerns the Parisi variational distribution and its quantile. It supplies no learning objective, training procedure, or generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constrained comparison attains a minimum and controls its optimized envelope analytically. Attainment does not supply a finite solver, an approximation guarantee, or a computational stopping criterion. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The boundary-test calculus preserves resampling estimates for products of overlaps under fixed topology and scalar centers. It gives no distributed coordination, consistency, or fault-recovery construction. |
| other (Probability and statistical mechanics) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper gives a concrete probabilistic construction: add an independent exponential variable to the completed log partition function. The resulting log-concave density permits the stated moment and density estimates to connect the positive Laplace window with fluctuation magnitudes. This is an analytic construction whose numerical evaluation is not supplied. |

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-reg-finite-propagation",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-reg-finite-propagation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* *Fix the bounds and recursion depth.* Enlarge the initial total-clock bounds by one to allow for descendant field additions. Use these enlarged bounds when choosing the constants in the limiting profiles. Choose $c,C$ strictly outside the bounds of all the limiting profiles described at the start of this section, with additional room near mass one. Fix $$N=\\left\\lceil\\frac{10}{\\delta}\\right\\rceil.$$ Before taking $n\\to\\infty$, choose $c_0>0$ small enough that $Nc_0^2<1$ and $Nc_0^2$ is less than half each initial fixed residual matrix lower bound or gap. It may also be decreased to meet the fixed bounds needed in the direct tests, including $c_0^2\\le T-\\rho$. Every child has $d'=c_0x\\le c_0$, and hence $d'^2\\le T-\\rho$. The sum of all further matrix removals along a chain of length $N$ is therefore at most $Nc_0^2$. All field additions are increasing and have total size at most $Nc_0^2$. Thus the total clock bounds and the covariance alternatives of Definition <a href=\"#reg:families\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"reg:families\">48</a> persist along this chain. In particular every child endpoint, every feasible child path, and every segment used in Lemma <a href=\"#reg:admissible-movement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"reg:admissible-movement\">50</a> are admissible.\n\n*A failed parent profile forces a failed child error.* Assume first that the profile assertion fails along a sequence. At its selected strength choose an offending mass $x\\ge A(\\lambda)$ within a factor two of the supremum of all offending masses. There are no offending masses above $2x$, so the path has a regular outer region above $x$. The numerical direct-test conditions $x\\gtrsim a$ and $e(x)x^2\\gtrsim nE^2$ follow from <a href=\"#eq:8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:8\">[eq:8]</a>. If $e(x)/x^2$ has a positive lower bound along a subsequence, Theorem <a href=\"#prof:direct-profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:direct-profile\">39</a> or Proposition <a href=\"#prof:macro-profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:macro-profile\">47</a>, according to the scale, immediately contradicts the choice of the loose constants $c,C$. After subselection the only remaining case is $e(x)/x^2\\to0$. Lemma <a href=\"#reg:child-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"reg:child-data\">49</a> constructs a child from the changed parent endpoint. If its error estimate were successful, Lemma <a href=\"#reg:successful-child\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"reg:successful-child\">52</a> would again contradict the parent violation. Hence a profile failure forces a child error failure.\n\n*A failed error selects the next profile failure.* Every such child has an admissible bare endpoint. If all its minimizers at all relevant strengths had the asserted profile, Proposition <a href=\"#comp:conditional-error\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:conditional-error\">25</a> and the optimized envelope of Lemma <a href=\"#comp:envelope\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:envelope\">16</a> would give $$\\mathcal V(d'^2)-f_n(b'_0,h'_0)\n       \\le O(\\log n)n^{10\\delta}E'+O(n^{-10})\n       <n^{20\\delta}E'$$ eventually. Strengths below $n^{-10}$ contribute $O(n^{-10})$ by bounded overlaps; when $A(\\lambda)$ is macroscopic the conditional error bound is unconditional. The derivative bound uses an old minimizer for positive increments, or equivalently the Lipschitz envelope inequality, and needs no monotone selection of minimizers. It follows that a child error failure selects a strength and a minimizing quantile with a profile failure. The same argument applies if the initial failure was an error failure at an ordinary endpoint. The lower bound in the proposition is the nonnegative segment interpolation in <a href=\"#eq:6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:6\">[eq:6]</a>.\n\n*The error parameter cannot grow for the whole fixed chain.* We may therefore repeat the construction: each bad profile forces a bad error for a valid child; that bad error selects the next bad profile. Lemma <a href=\"#reg:child-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"reg:child-data\">49</a> preserves all asymptotic endpoint hypotheses and multiplies $E$ by at least $n^{\\delta/4}$ at each stage. All choices of constants have already been made. At most $N$ successive subsequence extractions are involved, so the eventual inequalities can be imposed together. On the other hand <a href=\"#eq:3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:3\">[eq:3]</a>, at every stage, confines the error parameter to $$c_j n^{-5/6+2m}\\le E_j\\le C_j n^{-1/2},$$ where $c_j,C_j$ are fixed constants for that stage. A chain of $N$ increases would imply $E_N\\ge n^{N\\delta/4}E_0$, contradicting these bounds since $N\\delta/4\\ge5/2>1/3-2m$. This proves both assertions.\n\nFor a fixed strength root, the argument begins directly with its bad profile. There is no root sweep. Its first child starts from the admissible changed path $P_r$, and every subsequent error sweep has an admissible bare endpoint. Lemma <a href=\"#reg:admissible-movement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"reg:admissible-movement\">50</a> shows explicitly that no bare root pressure is needed. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-reg-finite-propagation"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-finite-low-temperature** (hypothesis): The fluctuation exponent concerns the zero-field Gaussian model at fixed finite low temperature. It supplies no uniform conclusion at either temperature endpoint. Blocked use: ['Extrapolating the fluctuation exponent to critical-temperature windows or ground-state fluctuations.']
- **limit-exponent-without-distribution-or-prefactor** (formal_scope): The exponent theorem does not identify a limiting distribution or a variance prefactor. Blocked use: ['Selecting a numerical limiting distribution or an asymptotic variance constant from the exponent theorem.']
- **limit-gaussian-model-scope** (hypothesis): The Hamiltonian uses independent standard Gaussian couplings and the specified spin interaction. Transfer to other disorder laws or interaction geometries is not established here. Blocked use: ['Applying the fluctuation theorem directly to non-Gaussian couplings, sparse interactions, or lattice models.']
- **limit-unconditioned-zero-field-pinned-moments** (hypothesis): The pinned spin moment inequality requires zero deterministic external field, deterministic weights, and the full unconditioned single-path planted law. Blocked use: ['Applying the moment estimate after conditioning on a realized prefix.', 'Replacing deterministic weights by random factors from another branch.', 'Using the same pinned estimate with a deterministic external field.']
- **limit-projection-conditioning** (hypothesis): The exterior anchor must leave the selected branch with its entire normalized planted future. Shared conditional means alone do not establish this condition. Blocked use: ['Constructing the anchor from unrevealed variables of the selected branch.', 'Applying the projection bound after conditioning on selected future observations.']
- **limit-signed-attachment-cancellation** (formal_scope): A small enclosing mass does not provide a saving for every ungrouped attachment term. Exact row collapse requires independence from the current row's detailed placement. Blocked use: ['Taking absolute values before the signed attachment contributions have been grouped.', 'Collapsing a row while its current coefficient still depends on deeper placement.']
- **limit-fixed-tests-and-bounded-row-differences** (hypothesis): Covariance interpolation must remain positive semidefinite with a uniformly bounded covariance difference. Parameter-dependent tests, centers, and deterministic weights require their product-rule terms. Blocked use: ['Transferring row estimates through an unbounded covariance change.', 'Differentiating a changing test while retaining only covariance-insertion derivatives.']
- **limit-fixed-size-path-approximation** (approximation): The bounded-path extension is a fixed-size limit argument. It requires convergence of terminal clocks and specified cut values, and does not supply a discretization rate uniform in physical size. Blocked use: ['Treating step approximation as a certified numerical tolerance uniform over growing systems.', 'Passing to path limits without retaining the specified sides of jumps.']
- **limit-prescribed-joint-jump-filling** (edge_case): Separate driver contributions and intermediate revelations inside a jump retain the prescribed joint clock filling. Only the total susceptibility increment is independent of that filling. Blocked use: ['Changing the joint filling while assuming separate clock contributions remain identical.']
- **limit-attainment-without-computational-certificate** (nonconstructive): Compactness and continuity establish attainment of the constrained minimum. The stated result does not supply a finite minimization procedure with a certified stopping criterion. Blocked use: ['Treating minimizer existence as an executable solver or a finite approximation guarantee.']
- **limit-local-regularity-before-propagation** (hypothesis): The local comparison error requires outer regularity when its cutoff tends to zero. The small direct-test results additionally retain their local strength and endpoint hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Using the conditional comparison error to establish its own unproved regularity premise.', 'Applying small-scale rigidity merely because the comparison objective has a similar form.']
- **limit-cap-averages-and-radius-control** (formal_scope): Whole-cap trace bounds are averaged estimates. Small floor mass does not imply small radius length; profile arguments require direct control in both coordinates. Blocked use: ['Replacing a whole-cap average by a pointwise trace bound.', 'Discarding a radius interval solely because its physical mass is negligible.']
- **limit-cavity-test-family-and-full-clock** (hypothesis): The cavity calculation retains the admissible boundary-test family, fixed topology coefficients and centers, and the entire scalar effective covariance path. Blocked use: ['Introducing internal overlap factors without re-establishing the boundary resampling argument.', 'Replacing the scalar continuation by a function of only its target clock value.']
- **limit-fixed-orders-and-recursion-depth** (hypothesis): Replica counts, polynomial degrees, derivative orders, moment orders, and recursion depths are fixed before the system size tends to infinity. Blocked use: ['Increasing the test degree or recursion depth with system size while keeping the stated bounds.']
- **limit-modified-constraints-need-new-proof-obligations** (formal_scope): Changing the inverse-density constraints does not automatically preserve minimizing-path closure or profile rigidity. Attainment, admissibility, first variation, feasible competitors, and the analytic error estimates must be established for the new optimizer. Blocked use: ['Importing closure or rigidity into a modified variational problem based only on resemblance of objectives.']
- **limit-ordinary-sweep-versus-fixed-strength-root** (hypothesis): The full comparison-excess bound belongs to ordinary families with an admissible bare endpoint. A fixed-strength family receives the profile conclusion without evaluation of an inadmissible bare root. Blocked use: ['Sweeping a wall root from zero strength when its bare field is inadmissible.', 'Applying an ordinary-family excess estimate directly to a fixed-strength root.']
- **limit-propagation-covariance-margins** (hypothesis): Finite propagation retains the specified budget range, endpoint expansion, omitted-mass constraint, bounded total clocks, and a positive covariance margin available throughout the descendant chain. Blocked use: ['Running descendant comparisons after the available matrix lower bound or boundary gap has been exhausted.', 'Removing the fixed positive power slack from the comparison data.']
- **limit-wall-parameter-range** (hypothesis): The wall profile and sweep costs use the prescribed lower wall, omitted group, and scale choices. Their uniformity over wall parameters does not remove those conditions. Blocked use: ['Using the wall estimates for an arbitrary field discontinuity or an unconstrained lower wall.']
- **limit-positive-transform-needs-distributional-control** (formal_scope): A positive Laplace window alone does not control variance or typical magnitude. The argument first establishes log-concavity after independent exponential augmentation and then uses the associated moment and density estimates. Blocked use: ['Inferring typical fluctuations directly from positive-transform growth.', 'Assuming the unaugmented free energy has the log-concave density proved for the augmented variable.']
- **limit-fixed-auxiliary-exponent** (hypothesis): The auxiliary exponent is fixed before the size limit. Constants and thresholds may depend on that choice. Blocked use: ['Replacing the fixed auxiliary exponent by a size-dependent sequence without additional estimates.']
- **limit-sequential-temperature-extension** (hypothesis): The sequential temperature extension holds a common prelimit temperature fixed through each parent–child construction, retains all margins, and requires a limiting temperature strictly above the transition. Blocked use: ['Assigning different prelimit temperatures to descendants in the same construction.', 'Claiming uniform estimates as the limiting temperature approaches the transition.']

For the exact collapse of deeper placements this coefficient, and all scalar centers in the row test, must be independent of $r$’s detailed attachment beyond the truncation cut. Allocation-order symmetry carries the full topology coefficient with it; it does not establish the independence required for a free-row collapse. At each recursion generation, freeze the previously generated topology, its coefficients, and earlier scalar centers before introducing fresh slots. A current coefficient depending on the row’s deeper placement must remain explicit until eliminated or estimated. Only after this row calculation may the enlarged topology be frozen for the next generation.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at every fixed inverse temperature <i>β</i> &gt; 1, we prove that the standard deviation of the log partition function is $`n^{1/6+o(1)}`$. The same exponent describes its typical centered absolute fluctuations, establishing the predicted one-sixth exponent in this regime.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $(g_{ij})_{1\le i<j\le n}$ be independent standard Gaussian random variables. For a fixed inverse temperature $\beta>1$, define $$H_n(\sigma)=\frac{\beta}{\sqrt n}\sum_{1\le i<j\le n}g_{ij}\sigma_i\sigma_j,
 \qquad Z_n=2^{-n}\sum_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^n}e^{H_n(\sigma)},
 \qquad F_n=\log Z_n.$$ The normalization of the spin sum changes $F_n$ by a deterministic constant and therefore has no effect on centered fluctuations.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every fixed $1<\beta<\infty$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{sd}}(F_n)=n^{1/6+o(1)},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(F_n)=n^{1/3+o(1)}.$$ More explicitly, for every $\eta>0$ there is $n_0(\beta,\eta)$ such that $$n^{1/6-\eta}\le\mathop{\mathrm{sd}}(F_n)\le n^{1/6+\eta}
 \qquad(n\ge n_0(\beta,\eta)).$$ Moreover, for every $\eta>0$, $$\mathbb P\!\left(n^{1/6-\eta}\le|F_n-\mathbb EF_n|
                         \le n^{1/6+\eta}\right)\longrightarrow1.$$*

</div>

For the free-energy density $F_n/n$, the standard-deviation and typical-magnitude exponents are $-5/6$, and the variance exponent is $-5/3$. Multiplication by the fixed physical factor $-1/\beta$ has no effect on these exponents. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> concerns power exponents; it gives neither a limiting law nor a nonzero constant prefactor. The temperature is fixed throughout, with no uniform conclusion as $\beta$ approaches $1$ or infinity.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and significance

The Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model (Sherrington and Kirkpatrick 1975) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[SK1975](#ref-SK1975)] separates two questions: the limiting free energy and its fluctuations between disorder samples. Parisi introduced the hierarchy of order parameters underlying the variational prediction for the first (Parisi 1979) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Parisi1979](#ref-Parisi1979)]. Guerra and Toninelli (Guerra and Toninelli 2002) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[GuerraToninelli2002](#ref-GuerraToninelli2002)] established the thermodynamic limit by Gaussian interpolation, and Guerra’s variational bound (Guerra 2003) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Guerra2003](#ref-Guerra2003)] and Talagrand’s matching result (Talagrand 2006) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Talagrand2006](#ref-Talagrand2006)] identified its Parisi value. Panchenko subsequently established the formula for general mixed $p$-spin models, including odd interactions (Panchenko 2014) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Panchenko2014](#ref-Panchenko2014)]. These results determine the leading extensive term; the fluctuations studied here occur on a smaller scale.

The prediction of that scale has a separate history. Kondor’s replica expansion near the critical temperature (Kondor 1983) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Kondor1983](#ref-Kondor1983)] is the starting point of the calculation described by Parisi and Rizzo (Parisi and Rizzo 2008, 2009) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ParisiRizzo2008](#ref-ParisiRizzo2008), [ParisiRizzo2009](#ref-ParisiRizzo2009)]. Crisanti, Paladin, Sommers, and Vulpiani (Crisanti et al. 1992) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CPSV1992](#ref-CPSV1992)] predicted a scale $n^{-5/6}$ for the physical free energy per spin in the low-temperature, zero-field phase. At fixed $\beta$, this corresponds to $n^{1/6}$ for the log partition function. Parisi and Rizzo developed the associated rare-deviation calculation through the low-temperature phase. Inferring typical fluctuations from that calculation requires an additional matching assumption, discussed in (Parisi and Rizzo 2009, sec. VI) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[ParisiRizzo2009](#ref-ParisiRizzo2009)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> establishes the predicted one-sixth exponent with arbitrarily small power slack, for every fixed finite $\beta>1$.

At fixed high temperature $0<\beta<1$, order-one Gaussian fluctuations go back to Aizenman, Lebowitz, and Ruelle (Aizenman, Lebowitz, and Ruelle 1987) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[AizenmanLebowitzRuelle1987](#ref-AizenmanLebowitzRuelle1987)]. Chatterjee’s work on disorder chaos and superconcentration (Chatterjee 2009, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Chatterjee2009](#ref-Chatterjee2009)] gives $\operatorname{Var}(F_n)=O_\beta(n/\log n)$, and his later lower-bound method proves constant-scale nonconcentration (Chatterjee 2019, Theorem 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Chatterjee2019LowerBounds](#ref-Chatterjee2019LowerBounds)]. Chen and Lam obtained a critical-temperature variance upper bound of order $(\log n)^2$ (Chen and Lam 2019) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ChenLam2019Critical](#ref-ChenLam2019Critical)]. At the critical point, Du and Huang subsequently proved variance $\frac16\log n+O(1)$ and a Gaussian limit (Du and Huang 2026, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[DuHuang2026Critical](#ref-DuHuang2026Critical)]. Critical-window limit theorems concern temperatures approaching the transition (Cheng et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[ChengLiuShaoXu2026CriticalWindow](#ref-ChengLiuShaoXu2026CriticalWindow)].

For the fixed low-temperature regime considered here, Aronow and Lopatto (Aronow and Lopatto 2026, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[AL2026](#ref-AL2026)] prove, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$c_\beta n^{4/15}\le \operatorname{Var}(F_n)
                         \le C_\beta n^{7/15}.$$ Their positive-transform and deviation estimates are a direct antecedent of the present argument.

The structure of the scalar Parisi minimizer is also essential. Auffinger and Chen established origin-support and regularity properties (Auffinger and Chen 2015a) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[AuffingerChenProperties2015](#ref-AuffingerChenProperties2015)], and proved uniqueness through a stochastic-control representation (Auffinger and Chen 2015b) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[AuffingerChenUniqueness2015](#ref-AuffingerChenUniqueness2015)]. Jagannath and Tobasco developed variational support and self-consistency conditions (Jagannath and Tobasco 2017) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[JagannathTobasco2017](#ref-JagannathTobasco2017)]. After Zhou’s interval-support result near the critical temperature (Zhou 2026) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Zhou2025](#ref-Zhou2025)], Lopatto (Lopatto 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Lopatto2026](#ref-Lopatto2026)] established interval support $[0,q_\beta]$ and the corresponding density structure at every fixed $\beta>1$. We use this theorem together with the support self-consistency in (Lopatto 2026, Lemma 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Lopatto2026](#ref-Lopatto2026)]. The strictly positive initial density needed here is then derived from scalar diffusion identities and a strict fourth-derivative estimate in Section <a href="#sec:profile" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:profile">5</a>.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy and method ancestry

Set $T=\beta^2$. We work with the completed covariance $$\mathbb E[H_n^c(\sigma)H_n^c(\tau)]=\frac{nT}{2}R(\sigma,\tau)^2,
 \qquad R(\sigma,\tau)=\frac{\sigma\cdot\tau}{n}.$$ Here $H_n^c=H_n+D$, where $D$ is an independent common Gaussian of variance $T/2$, and $F_n^c=F_n+D$. This completion gives the displayed quadratic covariance; the added noise is removed in the final step.

The quantitative target is a positive Laplace window for $\log\mathbb E\exp\{s(F_n^c-\mathbb EF_n^c)\}$ when $s$ exceeds $n^{-1/6}$ by an arbitrarily small fixed power of $n$ (Proposition <a href="#fl:laplace-window" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fl:laplace-window">59</a>). To compare these transforms, we reveal Gaussian covariance in stages and integrate a stage at mass $u$ by the operation $X\mapsto u^{-1}\log\mathbb E e^{uX}$, with expectation at $u=0$. Nondecreasing paths specify the cumulative interaction and external-field covariance. Copies of the spin system can share selected stages of this revelation; their prescribed common prefixes form the finite trees used in the proof.

This hierarchical framework belongs to the lineage of Parisi recursion, Ruelle’s cascades (Ruelle 1987) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Ruelle1987](#ref-Ruelle1987)], and Gaussian interpolation (Guerra and Toninelli 2002; Guerra 2003) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[GuerraToninelli2002](#ref-GuerraToninelli2002), [Guerra2003](#ref-Guerra2003)]. The cavity variational principle of Aizenman, Sims, and Starr (Aizenman, Sims, and Starr 2003) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[AizenmanSimsStarr2003](#ref-AizenmanSimsStarr2003)] is a further antecedent for comparisons that remove or add sites. We construct the finite positive transition laws used here directly.

The closest analytic predecessor is Aronow and Lopatto’s combination of a pinned Brascamp–Lieb variance estimate, optimized covariance interpolation, and positive moments (Aronow and Lopatto 2026, Propositions 2.2–2.3 and Section 3) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[AL2026](#ref-AL2026)]. The extensions proved here concern paired interaction and field clocks, local comparisons, fixed higher moments, and grouped replica tests at the fluctuation scale. The continuous inverse-Hessian inequality is due to Brascamp and Lieb (Brascamp and Lieb 1976) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BL1976](#ref-BL1976)]. Its application after pinning one terminal spin configuration is proved in Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">2</a>; the resulting moment estimate is for the full unconditioned planted path, not for an arbitrary fixed prefix.

A global replacement of both hierarchical pressures by their scalar Parisi limits loses too much at this scale. Instead, Section <a href="#sec:comparisons" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparisons">3</a> optimizes short covariance comparisons over a nondecreasing overlap quantile. The resulting error consists of overlap variance and the mismatch between that quantile and the mean overlap. Under comparability of mass and overlap above the scale being tested, projection and pinned moment estimates make these errors small in every fixed moment at selected split levels. The polynomial saving can be chosen independently of that fixed moment order.

Section <a href="#sec:cavity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cavity">4</a> turns these estimates into a scalar description of the mean overlap. Removing one spin produces a susceptibility correction that is too large under an absolute term-by-term bound. Additional test replicas give a finite system of identities whose iteration cancels that correction. For a block already containing observed replicas, the signed attachment contributions are summed before absolute values are taken. The number of test factors and the iteration depth remain fixed as $n$ grows. This gives the cubic one-site closure of Proposition <a href="#prop:cavity-closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cavity-closure">29</a>, including the uniform version needed for moving split locations.

Section <a href="#sec:profile" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:profile">5</a> uses that closure and the comparison’s first variation to determine its small-mass profile. A limiting scalar equation and a nonnegative variational potential exclude gaps in the rescaled overlap distribution, forcing a positive linear profile. The estimates hold in both mass and overlap coordinates, so an interval of negligible mass cannot conceal a gap of appreciable overlap length. The same section identifies macroscopic limits with the unique Parisi minimizer; its control proof follows the strict-convexity method of Auffinger and Chen (Auffinger and Chen 2015b) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[AuffingerChenUniqueness2015](#ref-AuffingerChenUniqueness2015)].

The local arguments assume regularity above the scale being tested. Section <a href="#sec:regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:regularity">6</a> removes this assumption by finite propagation. A failure is examined at a scale with a regular outer region. Sufficient comparison strength there already contradicts the direct profile result. In the remaining case, a stronger local comparison is constructed. If its error bound holds, agreement with the original comparison yields the same contradiction; if it fails, the next comparison has an error budget larger by a fixed power of $n$. A fixed number of repetitions exhausts the admissible range. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-order">1</a> records this alternative.

<figure id="fig:proof-order">

<figcaption>A selected bad parent scale has a regular outer region. The diagram concerns the remaining weak-strength case; a direct profile test already excludes sufficient local strength. Child-error success gives agreement. At a scale tending to zero, this supplies child outer regularity for the small-scale direct test; at a macroscopic scale, the Parisi profile applies instead. Either profile contradicts the parent failure (Lemma <a href="#reg:successful-child" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:successful-child">52</a>). Child-error failure selects a strength and minimizer, and then the next bad scale. Each child raises the error-budget parameter by a fixed power of system size. The covariance budget preserves admissibility, while this growth exhausts the allowed error range after a fixed number of steps (Proposition <a href="#reg:finite-propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reg:finite-propagation">53</a>). This propagation is separate from the local finite-degree cavity recursion.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-proof-order"></a>
